# Package C — mob AI parity (implemented)

- `Group+0x1D` activity: `0` idle, `1` combat, `0x20` return-to-rect. Shipped Game.N groups author `0x80`; mode-0 `FUN_0043ADF0` clears only `class & 0x40` (object/enemy) groups. `FUN_00412C30` runs every 16 logic frames for current-location `class & 0x40` groups:
  - any party member inside rectangle A -> `Group+0x1A = party group (0)`, `Group+0x1D = 1`;
  - else any own member outside rectangle A -> `0x20`;
  - else `0`.
- `FUN_004245B4` wake is activity `1` + clear `Entity+0x14` for `flags_a & 1 == 0` and `archetype != 0x49`; it does not assign a target.
- `FUN_00412E00` activity `0x20` is return/recall, not generic patrol.
- Target acquisition (`app/combat/ai.rs`): shared target (`FUN_0041FF68`), nearest unengaged (`FUN_00420190`), nearest (`F
UN_00420098`), 8-neighbour for `0x44/0x57`; per-archetype dispatch from `FUN_00412E00`. Existing `update_world_combat` handles row 5 / row 4 animation, movement and damage.
- `CombatTarget::World` now resolves cell/dead state, enabling world-vs-world combat.
- Citizen groups preserve current wander behavior; actors with `combat_target` are skipped by `update_population` so attack frames are not reset. Citizen combat activates when `wake_group` sets activity `1`.
- M/O walk delay (`FUN_004123BC`): archetypes `0x4D/0x4F` use `Entity+0x12` as a countdown at each walk-cycle boundary; ceiling is authored `Entity+0xD7`, read via `Res::entity_raw_d7`. Humanoid `+0x12` remains the active weapon slot.
- No OPKS schema bump: `Group.target_group` is `#[serde(skip)]` and rehydrated from `Game.N` on lo
ad; `activity_1d` remains in `SaveExtra`.
- Tests: `app/tests/combat_ai.rs` (7 cases), `tools/mob_ai_smoke.mjs` (group activity -> acquisition -> row 5 -> player damage). Full suite 384 passed.
- Evidence: `FUN_00412C30`, `FUN_004245B4`, `FUN_00412E00`, `FUN_004123BC`, `FUN_004168A4`, `FUN_0041FF68`, `FUN_00420098`, `FUN_00420190`, `FUN_0043ADF0`.

Package F3 positional audio implemented (web/src/sound/position.rs, SoundEvent, ABI sound_pan/sound_volume/sound_rate, app.js). FUN_00438DC4 pan: 0 when source_y == player_y else round_ties_even((source_y-player_y)*10000*0.00625) = 62.5/cell (FPU FISTP nearest-even). FUN_00438E24 volume: project both cells with FUN_00438A78 (=coords::entity_to_local_px), dx=player_x-source_x, dy=player_y-source_y, dist=round_ties_even(sqrt(dx^2+dy^2)); if dist>1600 -> -10000 else dist*-10000/1600 (IDIV truncation); FUN_0042A13C drops volume < -4000. FUN_00429C0C buffers: index 0=22050, 1=19050, 2=25050; FUN_0042A13C uses buffer 0 for id<0x20 except ids 2,3,6,7,8, else rand()%3. Remake queue: PlayableApp.sounds Vec<SoundEvent{id,volume,pan,rate}>; queue_sound (centred, still rolls rate) and queue_sound_at (
skips the roll when inaudible); actor action/melee/dodge queued at the actor cell; UI/item/voice/ambience centred. WebAudio: StereoPanner pan/10000, Gain 10^(volume/2000), playbackRate rate/base.

Package F2 character-screen requirements implemented (web/src/character/class.rs + character_screen.rs). Table 0x45B0AB = 5 class records x 0x68, each 4 stat blocks (order dexterity +0x00, charisma +0x1A, strength +0x34, endurance +0x4E), each block 13 u16 levels; low byte = growth cap (compared as 'cap <= current -> FUN_00410B08 returns 0'), high byte = per-level minimum for FUN_00410B08(entity,0). Class index = (Entity+0x04 & 7)-1 (Merchant 2 -> 1, Hunter 3 -> 2, Chief 4 -> 3, Warrior 5 -> 4). Normal cap ramp 255,7,10,14,18,22,26,30,32,35,37,39,41 and min ramp 3,5,8,12,16,20,24,28,31,34,36,38,40; specialized cap ramp 150,19,26,34,43,53,63,74,80,90,95,97,100 and min ramp 10,16,22,30,38,48,58,68,77,85,92,96,98 (some level-0 caps are 120 or 150 per class; full tables in STAT_GROWTH_CAPS/STA
T_GROWTH_MINS). Raise action (FUN_0042260C commands 2/3/5/6): cost = first STAT_TIERS threshold > current with specialization; +0x38 available_xp -= cost, +0x3C invested_xp += cost, current half +1. Command 0 = level up: gate FUN_00410B08(entity,0) (all four currents >= level minima, level 0xD refused), then +0x33 += 1, no XP cost. Icon-only row 0 uses INTR[171] (10x10 plus) when the gate passes; rows 1..4 draw the cost number at 0x465B34 anchors and the icon at x+0x18, y+2. Fixed pre-existing class mapping bug: 2=Merchant, 3=Hunter, 4=Chief, 5=Warrior.

after 16 retries of an unacknowledged packet.

### 3.7 UI / item / character gates

| gate | global | meaning |
|---|---|---|
| menu role | `0x7577E4` | client vs host; invalidates the first two menu items for clients |
| interface mode | `0x757800` | world / map / inventory-detail branches |
| modal flags | `0x75780C` | bits 0/1/2/3 gate input and interface requests |
| video depth | `0x7578A0` | 16-bit vs 32-bit sprite conversion |
| options | `0x7577CC`, `0x7577E0`, `0x7577F0`, `0x7577F8`, `0x757810`, `0x757818` | six settings |
| map id | `0x6EB7B0` | map selection and terrain scan |
| clan count | `0x6EB7A4` | `CLANS.CFG` line count |
| mouse | `0x7577AC`, `0x7577B0` | button state/edge |
| key enable | `0x7577E8` | suppress key enqueue |

## 5. Verification notes and limits

- The catalogue is a static direct-branch inventory. It does not model indirect
  jumps other than the 17 detected tables, and it does not prove reachability.
- Conditions are attached from the immediately preceding flag-setting
  instruction. In hand-written assembly that is reliable in the sampled
  functions; a branch whose flags come from a call or a farther producer will
  show an empty or stale `flag_source`.
- `jmp-table` rows carry the table address in `target`; the index source is in
  `globals` when it is an absolute global.
- Packet-type and quest-opcode semantics remain partial; see
  `CLIENT_EXE_NETWORK.md` and `CLIENT_EXE_SAVE_LOAD_QUEST.md`.

## 4. How to read a gate row

Example (from `FUN_0042CA4F`, packet venue removal):

```text
address=0x42ca93  function=0x42ca4f  mnemonic=je  target=0x42cb01
flag_source=0x42ca90  flag_mnemonic=test  flag_operands=byte ptr [eax], 0x80
globals=0x5baae0
```

This means: at `0x42CA93`, jump to `0x42CB01` when the byte at `[eax]` does
**not** have bit `0x80`; otherwise the venue-removal block runs.

# Konung multiplayer client — data files and configuration formats

Target: `CLIENT.EXE` (PE32/i386, base `0x400000`,
`sha256 adf0a2793ee42b16dcf31c53799cf5c9b0ca37471873e1888c26e2b5f01bf4ac`).
All addresses are `CLIENT.EXE`-specific.

The client does **not** use the single-player data model (`Game.0/1/2`,
`Konung.N`, `Lights.N`, `QUEST_R.RES`). It reads packed RES archives and a
124-byte `KONUNG.CFG`. This document records every loader and the exact byte
layout recovered from the loaders, plus the run-time archive names that are
built dynamically and therefore invisible to a string search.

## 1. File set

## 4. `CLANS.CFG` — up to 256 × 32 bytes

`FUN_004172FF`:

```c
count = 0;
memset(0x72C26C, 0, 0x2000);          // 256 × 0x20
for each line:
    read_line(buf, 0x20);
    trim; skip leading spaces;
    if (buf[0] == 0) break;
    count++;
    if (count >= 0x100) break;
count -> 0x6EB7A4
```

- Table: `0x72C26C`, stride `0x20`, max `0x100` entries.
- Used by `FUN_004169DA` to append one line per clan to the log before dying,
  and by packet `0x0C` (clan/player name string).

`<width>` is `_DAT_007578B0` (640 for mode `0x111`, 800 for `0x114`), formatted

`"ME" @ 0x4402F4` + itoa(width) + `"R" @ 0x4402F7` + `".RES" @ 0x4402F9`.
Without `ME640R.RES` the client logs
`"ME640R.RES not found rc=16"` and exits 1 (observed locally).

## 5. `SERVERS.CFG` — up to 100 × 80 bytes

`FUN_004172FF`:

```c
count = 0;
memset(0x6C899C, 0, 8000);            // 100 × 0x50
for each line:
    read_line(buf, 0x40);
    trim; skip leading spaces;
    if (buf[0] == 0) break;
    count++;
    if (count >= 100) break;
count -> 0x757898
```

- Table: `0x6C899C`, stride `0x50`, max 100 entries.
- Each entry is a connection spec consumed by `FUN_0042D1EC`; the code uses the
  first whitespace-delimited token only (stops at a character `<= 0x20`).

## 9. `OBJECTS.RES`

Loader: `FUN_0041BF8E`.

```text
read(fd, hdr, 8);
read(fd, table, 0x1000);               // 512 × {u32 offset,u32 size}

    table[i].offset += 0x1008;         // data base
    lseek(fd, table[i].offset, SEEK_SET);
    if i < 0x1E:
        table[i].offset += 0x3228;
        table[i].size  -= 0x3228;
        read(fd, 0x66A8EC + i*0x3228, 0x3228);
    else:
        table[i].offset += 0x100;
        table[i].size  -= 0x100;

If the file is missing, the six flags default to 1, the video mode to `0x111`,
and the three strings are zeroed. There is **no length check**; a short file
leaves the rest at BSS values. The single-player `KONUNG.CFG` is 28 bytes, so
the two formats are **not interchangeable**.

The three strings are also the source for the 0x73-byte player-state packet:
`FUN_0041701E` copies `0x7570B0` (32 B), `0x7570D0` (8 B) and `0x757038` (32 B)
into `0x6EB55C`.

`Maps.res` is 3 856 000 bytes = **20 × 0x2F120**, so the client ships 20 maps.
The raw collision block is converted to the runtime `u16` grid at `0x599CD8`
(320 × 160): `0xFF` → `0x0FFF`; low nibble `0xF` → `(b << 8) | 0x0FFF`; else
`b << 8`.

World objects are 100 × 0x40 at `0x577FD8 + map_id*0x1900`; active types are
marked in the collision grid with bit `0x20` (same convention as the
single-player build).

`ev_3c2afb50daadb9f93233b80357f76ee39d067f601d3a3c7f73b1b0739e5bd29d`).

```text
open(FLAMES.RES)
for i in 0..0x49:                       // 74 frames
    pointers[i] = 0x754BD8 + i*4;
    read(fd, hdr, 8);
    read(fd, hdr.data, hdr.size);
    if (option 0x7577CC) decompress(0x41D828, frame);
    else if (video_depth == 0x10) convert16(0x41DB30, frame);
    pointers[i] += size;
```

- 74 frames; pointer table `0x754BD8`.
- Optional RLE decompression (`0x41D828`) gated by option `0x7577CC`, or 16-bit
  conversion (`0x41DB30`) when the video depth is 16.

## 4. How to use the CSV

- `evidence/client_exe_functions.csv` is the authoritative address→label map.
  `role` is a reachability/region classification, not a semantic claim.
- `evidence/client_exe_gates.csv` is the branch catalogue. For a gate, read
  `flag_source`/`flag_mnemonic`/`flag_operands` for the condition and `target`
  for the taken edge; `globals` lists the absolute memory operands involved.

# Konung multiplayer client — DirectPlay network subsystem

Target: `CLIENT.EXE` (PE32/i386, base `0x400000`,
`sha256 adf0a2793ee42b16dcf31c53799cf5c9b0ca37471873e1888c26e2b5f01bf4ac`).
All addresses are `CLIENT.EXE`-specific.

This document describes the client's network layer: DirectPlay setup, session
join, the receive queue and packet dispatch, the reliable send queue, the
timeout/`Server failed` gate, and the runtime log. It is static evidence plus
one local unattended run; no server was available, so no packet exchange was
observed.

## 1. Imports and GUIDs

#### `drawer_konung_client-exe_1dd051a77d37f39979ddb0b1`

- **wing:** konung
- **room:** client-exe
- **source_file:** C:\Users\z10ba\AppData\Local\Temp\opencode\client_palace\client-exe\CLIENT_EXE_NETWORK.md
- **filed_at:** 2026-10-22T17:49:41.926080
- **added_by:** opencode
- **hall:** technical
- **chunk_index:** 4
- **chunk_total:** 20

ery the lobby interface for the lobby IID `0x440BCC`
   (`QueryInterface`-shaped call at slot `+0`).
6. `lobby->[+0x38](&connection, 2, &out, &size)` — connection/settings call
   (exact slot name unresolved; argument shape matches the lobby connection
   API).
7. Release the queried lobby view and the lobby object.
8. `CoInitialize(NULL)` and
   `CoCreateInstance(CLSID_DirectPlay, NULL, CLSCTX_INPROC_SERVER, IID, &dp)`
   — the interface pointer is stored at **`0x778630`**.
9. `dp->[+0x98](0x757180, 0)` — connection initialisation using the lobby
   connection blob at `0x757180`.
10. `dp->[+0x34](&session, 0, callback 0x42D7EC, hwnd, 1)` — session/enumeration

The function takes one string (a server/connection spec) and performs:

1. `DirectPlayEnumerateA(callback 0x42EAFC, hwnd)` — enumerate service
   providers. The callback sets `DAT_007786B6`/`DAT_007786B7` and stores the
   selected provider GUID at `0x77860C` (`FUN_0042D7EC`,
   `ev_749c943d15bba57e80b688639e6395f49a949fd534062bd0d5c98feb10b18d02`).
2. If no provider was found → return 0.
3. Copy the connection string into a 64-byte local buffer, stopping at the
   first character `<= 0x20` (space) — i.e. only the first token is used.
4. `DirectPlayLobbyCreateA(0, &lobby, 0, 0, 0)`.
5. Query the lobby interface for the lobby IID `0x440BCC`
   (`QueryInterface`-shaped call at slot `+0`).

— `FUN_0042CA4F` (`0x42CA4F`)

`ev_ebba0c83c293beae721cacce85befad11d8d276deb51110a9c17359ffc5ef6e6`

The receive buffer at `0x6EB154` holds a **stream of concatenated packets**:

```text
+0x00  u32  total_bytes_remaining
+0x04  u8   type            (low 6 bits = handler index; high bit 0x80 = venue close)
+0x05  ...  payload
```

The dispatcher:

```text
p = buffer + 4;
while (buffer->len != 0) {
    if (*p & 0x80) {                 // venue/player disconnect notification
        id = *(u16*)(p+1);
        remove the matching 0x20B venue record from 0x5BAAE0 and compact;
    }
    consumed = packet_handlers[*p & 0x3F](p);
    buffer->len -= consumed;
    p += consumed;
}
```

— `FUN_0041701E` (`0x41701E`)

`ev_d76d940afee8943d8c15594adea8bf383e37d3630e4b0c5c14221eef91a0e270`

Builds a **0x73-byte** record at `0x6EB55C` and sends it with
`FUN_004284A0(record, 0x73, 1)` (queued reliable):

```text
+0x00  u8   0x4C
+0x01  u8   player slot (0)
+0x03  ...  32-byte player name from 0x7570B0
+0x23  ...  8 bytes from 0x7570D0
+0x2B  ...  32 bytes from 0x757038
...
+0x4B  ...
```

It also sets `_DAT_006EB774 = 0`, `_DAT_006EB7AC = 0xA`, `_DAT_006EB7CE = 0xFF`
and copies many hero fields (`0x75758E..0x7575BF`) into the packet. This is the
client's player-state broadcast (used when entering the session).

`FUN_0041701E` is called from the session screen (state 5); its packet is the
best starting point for a future server-compatible protocol implementation.

## 7. Residual unknowns

1. Exact DirectPlay vtable slot names (IID is the 3A family, but the observed
   offsets do not all match the documented IDirectPlay3 order).
2. Semantics of 29 of the 30 packet types (`0x00..0x1D`); only `0x0C` is
   partially decoded.
3. Why the dispatcher masks type with `0x3F` when only 30 handler slots are
   code pointers; types `0x1E..0x3F` would call non-code data and are assumed
   never to be sent.
4. The server side (there is no server executable in the install; `SERVERS.CFG`
   is only a list of connection strings).
5. The lobby connection blob at `0x757180` (built from the `SERVERS.CFG`
   string) is not decoded.

# Konung multiplayer client — save/load status and quest system

Target: `CLIENT.EXE` (PE32/i386, base `0x400000`,
`sha256 adf0a2793ee42b16dcf31c53799cf5c9b0ca37471873e1888c26e2b5f01bf4ac`).
All addresses are `CLIENT.EXE`-specific.

This document answers two questions that motivated the investigation:

1. does `CLIENT.EXE` participate in the save/load system?
2. does it reuse the `QUEST_R.RES` quest engine?

The answers are: **no** for save/load, and **yes for the engine, no for the
script data**.

## 1. Save/load: absent

### 1.1 Four independent negative checks

- **filed_at:** 2026-10-22T17:49:42.555207
- **added_by:** opencode
- **hall:** technical
- **chunk_index:** 1
- **chunk_total:** 17

engine, no for the
script data**.

## 1. Save/load: absent

### 1.1 Four independent negative checks

| check | result |
|---|---|
| raw byte search for `konung.sa`, `.sav`, `sa0`, `save`, `load game`, `konung.0`, `game.0` (case-insensitive) | **0 occurrences** each |
| defined-string inventory (`eval_69f5e0e8…`) | no save/load file name, no slot text, no "save" string |
| file-name loaders | the only disk names are `CLANS.CFG`, `SERVERS.CFG`, `KONUNG.CFG`, `CLIENT.LOG`, `MAPS.RES`, `SOUNDS_.RES`, `GRAPH.RES`, `LIGHTS.RES`, `OBJECTS.RES`, `GAME.RES`, `QUESTR.RES`, `FLAMES.RES`, and `PLAY/ME/NEW/INTO<w>R.RES` |
| function inventory | no procedure with a save/load role; no `KONUNG.SAV` / `KONUNG.SA0..9` strings for a slot writer/reader |

#### `drawer_konung_client-exe_27f0c6fdfb2111b4d0762055`

rocedure with a save/load role; no `KONUNG.SAV` / `KONUNG.SA0..9` strings for a slot writer/reader |

The raw search is decisive: the single-player game's entire save format is
keyed on the literals `KONUNG.SAV` and `KONUNG.SA0..9`, and none of those
substrings exists anywhere in `CLIENT.EXE`.

### 1.2 What the client persists

| file | direction | content |
|---|---|---|
| `KONUNG.CFG` | read (writer candidate `0x42D000`/`0x42D064`) | 7 × u32 options + 3 × 0x20 strings (124 B) |
| `CLANS.CFG` | read only | clan/faction names (≤256 × 0x20) |
| `SERVERS.CFG` | read only | connection strings (≤100 × 0x50) |
| `CLIENT.LOG` | append | `<message> rc=<code>` diagnostic lines |

The world/party/quest state is **not** written to disk by this binary. It lives
in the DirectPlay session and in the process memory; the client is a thin
multiplayer front end, not a save-game owner. This is consistent with the
single-player `KONUNG.SA0..9` format (`KONUNG_SAVE_LOAD.md`), which is entirely
absent here.

### 1.3 Consequence for the remake

The remake's save/load contract must continue to be derived from `konung.res`
(`FUN_004246C0` reader / `FUN_00424B3C` writer, `KONUNG.SAVE_LOAD.md`).
`CLIENT.EXE` provides no additional save-format evidence.

### 2.3 Opcode dispatch — `FUN_00425E5C` (`0x425E5C`)

`ev_a69b113c79471376777bcea6230365b956defbac792a1c8d830d81e7cca5244b`

```c
int dispatch(opcode) {
    if (opcode < 0x38) return 0;
    if (opcode < 0x70) return 0;
    if (opcode < 0xA6) { FUN_004279B8(opcode - 0x0B); return FUN_00426B0A(); }
    if (opcode < 0xDC) { FUN_004279B8(opcode + 0x23); return FUN_00425F7F(); }
    if (opcode < 0x112) return FUN_00426B31(opcode - 0xDB);
    i = opcode - 0x112;
    if (table[i].has_text) FUN_004279B8(i);
    return table[i].handler();
}
```

— `FUN_004271A9` (`0x4271A9`)

`ev_870620e63d46c47a6cb40e20dd63d498b5fe04316fcb399bc0ff40c752d20fb1`

```c
void eval_conditions(page_index) {
    u16 *p = &B4[0x554CC8 + page_index*2];
    while (*p != 0xFFFF) {
        if ((*p & 0x2000) == 0) {
            flag = *p & 0x1FF;                 // 0x1FF-bit flag index
            if (*p & 0x8000) flag_array[flag] |= 0x80;
            else             flag_array[flag] ^= 0x80;
        } else {
            FUN_00425E5C(*p & 0x1FF);          // opcode predicate
        }
        p++;
    }
}
```

for it. Status: **partially inferred**.

## 3. Conclusion

- **Save/load:** not implemented in `CLIENT.EXE`. The single-player
  `KONUNG.SAV` + `KONUNG.SA0..9` system is the only save format in the install;
  the client is a DirectPlay session front end.
- **Quest:** same 7-block engine and same 93-opcode range, but a different
  (earlier/smaller) script in `QUESTR.RES` and a set of handlers that is only
  partially equivalent. The client is useful as a **second implementation
  witness** for the quest engine's structure, not as a replacement for the
  single-player dialogue data.

`0x425F58` is a shared no-op/return handler (used by 28 opcodes), so the
client implements a smaller active subset than the single-player build.

### 2.7 Quest flags

- `0x575068`, 300 records × 4 bytes (`0x4B0`).
- Condition terms use the low 9 bits as the flag index and bit `0x8000` as the
  set/clear selector; the runner toggles bit `0x80` in the record.
- This is the client's equivalent of the single-player `0x615068` quest-flag
  array (which is saved in `KONUNG.SA0..9`; the client's array is session
  state only).

## 4. Message/frame loop (`0x42EE??–0x42EF6F`)

```text
while (_DAT_007786B5) {                     // running
    if (PeekMessageA(&msg, 0, 0, 0, PM_REMOVE)) {
        TranslateMessage(&msg);
        DispatchMessageA(&msg);
    } else if (_DAT_007786B4) {             // application active
        now = timeGetTime();
        if (now - last_tick >= _DAT_006EB778) {   // 60 ms
            last_tick = now;
            SendMessageA(hwnd, 0x0400, 0, 0);     // WM_USER: one frame
        }
    }
}
```

# Konung multiplayer client — startup, WinMain, WndProc and frame loop

Target: `CLIENT.EXE` (PE32/i386, image base `0x400000`,
`sha256 adf0a2793ee42b16dcf31c53799cf5c9b0ca37471873e1888c26e2b5f01bf4ac`,
939 008 bytes, 538 procedure entries, 174 defined strings).

All addresses are **`CLIENT.EXE`-specific**. They must not be reused for
`konung.res`; the two binaries share the CRT/stub convention and the Win32
skeleton but have different code and data layouts.

This document is new and does not modify `KONUNG_STARTUP_MAIN.md` (which
describes the single-player `konung.res`). Companion documents:
`CLIENT_EXE_STATE_MACHINE.md`, `CLIENT_EXE_NETWORK.md`,
`CLIENT_EXE_DATA_FORMATS.md`, `CLIENT_EXE_SAVE_LOAD_QUEST.md`,
`CLIENT_EXE_FUNCTION_MAP.md`, `CLIENT_EXE_BRANCHES.md`.

The entry stub writes the WinMain pointer to `0x7786D4` and jumps to the CRT
startup. The CRT startup calls that pointer and exits with its return value.
This is the same pattern as `konung.res` (`0x43F4B4` → `0x44016C` →
`0x42B1A8`), with different addresses.

Differences from the single-player loop:

- There is **no movie mode**. The client does not import `PlayAVI.dll` and has
  no blocking `GetMessage` branch / `WM_USER+0x0D` movie pump.
- The loop is otherwise identical: event-driven, one `WM_USER 0x400` frame per
  60 ms while the window is active, no timer thread and no `Sleep`.

## 5. WndProc (`0x42EF70`, stdcall, `ret 0x10`)

Ghidra defines no function at `0x42EF70`; the body was decoded from bytes.
Argument layout: `hwnd=[ebp+0x14]`, `msg=[ebp+0x18]`, `wParam=[ebp+0x1C]`,
`lParam=[ebp+0x20]`.

This establishes two facts that are invisible in the defined-string inventory:

1. The client builds four archive names at run time:
   `PLAY<width>R.RES`, `ME<width>R.RES`, `NEW<width>R.RES`,
   `INTO<width>R.RES` (`width` = 640 or 800), from the pieces
   `"PLAY" / "ME" / "NEW" / "INTO"`, `"R"`, `".RES"` at `0x4402d0–0x44032b`.
2. `FUN_0041FF3F` is the loader for `ME<width>R.RES`; it is called from the
   initial transient state 9 before the main menu. On failure it logs
   `"<name> not found rc=16"` through `FUN_004169DA` and returns 0.

## 7. Shutdown

- Menu “Выход” calls the window close path; `FUN_0043BF5C`-equivalent sets
  `_DAT_006EB780`.
- The next WndProc call clears `_DAT_007786B5` and calls `DestroyWindow`.
- `WM_DESTROY` calls `FUN_0042D788`, `FUN_0042D69C`, `FUN_0042D174` and
  `PostQuitMessage(0)`, ending the loop.
- Local unattended run: a clean menu exit produced `CLIENT.LOG` containing
  `" rc=0"` and process exit code 0 (see §8).

## 6. Thread model

- The only `CreateThread` code reference is the import thunk at `0x439662`
  (`KERNEL32.DLL::CreateThread`), referenced from `0x438B1B` inside the
  CRT/library region. Run-time procedural graph shows no game function
  creating a thread.
- The client therefore runs a single game/UI thread. DirectPlay may create
  internal OS threads inside `DPLAYX.dll`; those are outside the shipped image
  and are not observed here.
- There is no PlayAVI helper thread (the import and all movie code are absent).

# Konung multiplayer client — screen state machine

Target: `CLIENT.EXE` (PE32/i386, base `0x400000`,
`sha256 adf0a2793ee42b16dcf31c53799cf5c9b0ca37471873e1888c26e2b5f01bf4ac`).
All addresses are `CLIENT.EXE`-specific.

This is the behavioural model of the shipped multiplayer client: the screen
states, their per-frame handlers, transitions, and the input routing that feeds
them. It is derived from the state jump table at `0x429F44`, the index tables of
each state handler, and a local unattended run that displayed the 640×480 main
menu (`Игра / Новый герой / Установки / Выход`).

## 2. State dispatch

`FUN_0042855E` (`0x42855E`, 6 407 bytes, non-contiguous) is the per-frame input
and state driver. It runs inside the `WM_USER 0x400` frame:

```text
FUN_0042D094():                 # network/queue pump (0x42D094)
    _DAT_006EB7C8++             # frame counter
    _DAT_006EB358 = 0
    FUN_0042855E()              # consume one input event + state handler
    drain <=15 network packets  # FUN_0042D574 / FUN_0042CA4F on 0x6EB154
    FUN_0042A00B()              # venue keepalive / "Server failed" gate
    if (_DAT_006EB358) FUN_0042D620()
FUN_0042E19C():                 # frame compositor/present (0x42E19C)
```

`FUN_0042855E` structure:

2. Update Shift/Ctrl modifier levels; ESC is handled here (see §5).
3. Latch mouse button edges into `_DAT_007577B0`; integrate the mouse delta
   (`FUN_0041EC52`) into the clamped cursor `_DAT_007577BC / _DAT_007577C0`.
4. Dispatch on `_DAT_006EB774` through the table at **`0x429F44`**:

## 4. Per-state handlers

### 4.1 State 0 — transition arbiter (`0x4288B5`, 54 bytes)

```text
if (_DAT_006EB7AC == 0xA && _DAT_006EB764 != 0) {
    _DAT_006EB774 = 0xA;
} else if (_DAT_006EB7BC == 0) {
    _DAT_006EB774 = _DAT_006EB7AC;      // commit the pending action
}
```

This is the only state that copies a pending action into the screen state, so
`0x6EB7AC` is the client's equivalent of a "next screen" code.

### 4.2 State 1 — gameplay (`0x4288EB`)

Calls (direct, in order): `0x432945` (rand), `0x42DE5C` (sound), `0x41BBA5`,
`0x41B7DF` (sprite blit), `0x4144BD`, `0x4147DF`, `0x411ECE`, `0x413479`
(world/actor passes), `0x4183A5`, `0x4184D8` (active lists), `0x42289F`,
`0x425812`, `0x427C52` (quest/interface), `0x414E11`, `0x41EE12`.

`FUN_0042255A` iterates **10 player slots** at `0x5E812C`, stride `0x74`
(116 bytes) — exactly the `0x488` bytes that the main-menu "Новый герой"
branch zero-initialises. Each slot holds a number, a name and several numeric
fields; the renderer uses `FUN_00433865` (itoa) and `FUN_0041B8D7` (text blit).

## 7. Invariants

- Exactly one screen state is active per frame; the handler runs once per
  `WM_USER 0x400`.
- Exactly one keyboard event is consumed per frame.
- Mouse state is sampled; button presses are latched into `0x7577B0` and
  consumed by the current state handler.
- State transitions are committed either directly (`_DAT_006EB774 = n`) or via
  the arbiter state 0 through `_DAT_006EB7AC`.
- Frame budget is 60 ms (`0x6EB778`).

### 4.10 State 9 — initial preload/transition (`0x429D6E`)

```text
_DAT_006EB774 = 3;                       // commit: next state is the main menu
_DAT_007577B8 = 0; _DAT_007577B4 = 0;
FUN_0041EF3E();
idx = FUN_00416F91(cursor, 0x4491D0);
if (_DAT_007577E4 == 0 && idx < 2) idx = -1;
if (_DAT_006EB7AC != 8) FUN_0041FF3F();  // load ME<width>R.RES
else                    FUN_00420A96(...); // alternate start path
```

`FUN_0041FF3F` builds `"ME" + itoa(_DAT_007578B0) + "R" + ".RES"`
(`0x4402F4`+`0x4402F7`+`0x4402F9`) — i.e. `ME640R.RES` or `ME800R.RES` — and
loads it into the interface buffers. It is the first archive the client needs;
without it the process logs `"ME640R.RES not found rc=16"` and exits 1.

Attack order FUN_0042260C: right-click non-dialogue actor, different group; for each selected unit 0x81A814 with Entity+0x03&4 (battle stance): order 0x44 'D' when +0x12==1 && +0x68!=0 && +0xCC!=0 else 0x41 'A'; target id to Entity+6; +0x14=0xFF. No destination; no move if no unit in stance.

Hit FUN_0041B060/B42C: rand()%101, hit when reaction >= roll. monster reaction=round(50+dex*0.25). human melee reaction=round(100-(1.5+melee*0.00015)/(100-dex*0.5)). human archery uses archery and bow range term.

Damage FUN_0041AEA8: monster=round(STR*HP/400); human unarmed=round(STR*HP/800); human armed slot 0/2 = weapon_value + round(STR*HP/800). weapon_value = 0x462798 modifier if instanc
e+0x02 matches target Entity+4 else prototype rating 0x459B7C+subtype*0x40. poison = weapon +0x0C or monster Entity+0x50.

Apply FUN_0041C3CC: armor=base_armor(+0x4E)+sum rating equipment slots 3..5. facing table 0x450DB4[defender.facing*8+attacker.facing]!=0 enables dodge when rand()%101<=round(dex*0.5). temp=max(0,incoming*0.7-armor); final=round(16*(1+(incoming*0.3+temp)/endurance)); lethal when final>=hp. defender +0x50 += poison. armor wear ratio = 1 if armor==0 else min(incoming/armor,1); slot3..5 durability -= ratio*rating*0.001. attacker weapon +0x0C decrement; durability -= incoming*0.0001. Break: class byte 0xFF, slot cleared.

Animation: melee resolves on last attack-animation frame (FUN_00413DE4 returns 0). death rows 3/9, object rows 6..11 alias 0..5; second-to-last death fram
e frees collision, final frame held. DOT every 16 logic frames hp -= periodic_damage (FUN_0041D048/FUN_0041BD3C).

Loot FUN_00418480: money Entity+0x34 + rand()%bonus (bonus>=2); up to 22 inventory ids Entity+0x72; if Entity+0x52!=0 add class 9 subtype 0 'Ядовитое жало' durability 1.0 instance+0x0C=+0x52; generated item only killer_level!=0 && rand()%101>85 (melee passes 0,0). branches: 86 class 0/1/2 subtype rand%(level/5+1)+(rand%9)*3 class from subtype%9; 87 class 3/5/4 subtype local+rand%3*11+0x22/0x25/0x28; 88..94 class8 +(6); 95..96 class7 +(3); 97..100 class6 +(0). tier FUN_00417144 level<4 3,<7 6,<10 8,else 10; modifier=rand()%tier+(rand%5)*10.

Correction: item damage-modifier index is ItemInstance +0x02, not +0x01. +0x01 is condition.

Remake: web/src/combat.rs, combat/loot.rs, a
pp/combat.rs, item.rs/res.rs. Ranged projectile action 4 deferred. Smoke: node tools/combat_smoke.mjs.

VERIFIED RANGED CONTRACT (konung.res d698c4f231489a372cc25212ab2819720a36d7ce5307a045db684135cf679810).

Bow Equipment[1] (Entity+0x68), active weapon +0x12==1. Ammo Entity+0xCC class12 stack qty +0x04 u32; one consumed at spawn; zero clears slot/class 0xFF. Bow +0x04 durability -0.1 per shot; break class 0xFF, Eq1=0. Action row 4 fires on frame count-6, no range/approach check.

Spawn FUN_0041BF44: start=EntityProjection(render_cell)+muzzle[facing]; target=EntityProjection(order cell), y-=30; fx=|dx|*0.25+0.001, fy=|dy|*0.25+0.001; v=(dx/fy,dy/fy) if fx<fy else /fx. life=prototype +0x08. sprite=184+2*facing(+1 odd frame). draw offsets 0x4580A0/0x4580A1. Muzzle X [-70,-48,12,68,80,52,-8,-60], Y [-65,-99,-85,-90,-58,-26,-10,-31].

Damage FUN_0041AC40/1C1E8: base=bow modifier value (instance
 +0x02) if target archetype matches else rating 0x459B7C+subtype*0x40; raw=base*bow_rating; dodge rand%101<=round(dex_base*0.5); armor=base_armor+slots3..5 ratings; temp=max(0,raw*0.7-armor); final=round(16*(1+(raw*0.3+temp)/endurance_base)); poison=bow+0x0C; armor wear min(raw/armor,1)*rating*0.001; no weapon wear on hit.

Flight FUN_00420F30: 8 substeps/frame, lifetime--/frame, blocked cell removes arrow; actor cell damages any non-owner living entity (friendly fire). Kill: death 3/9; owner money+=power/4, archery mastery+=power; corpse loot bonus=power, killer level=owner level. power=FUN_0041B658.

Prop branch: right-click prop target id=-(prop_index+1), destination clicked cell; only ammo instance +0x02==0. Blocked cell removes arrow; if point opaque on prop sprite (FUN_0043E4EA): ran
d%100<21 => settlement target +1=3,+6=1; settlement +0x4B2|=0x15; wake settlement group +0x02 (FUN_004245B4 activity/clear queues).

Arrow recovery NEGATIVE: fired arrow consumed at spawn; projectile/damage/loot code never re-adds it. Lootable arrows come from victim inventory via FUN_00418480. Game.1 entity 40 loc4 group4 carries item 102 class12 subtype30 qty30. 13 mobs carry class12 stacks. NPC archers carry bows but no ammo.

Remake: combat/projectile.rs, app/combat.rs, render prop hit-test, settlement targets. Smoke: node tools/ranged_smoke.mjs.

CLIENT.EXE data formats (all CLIENT.EXE-specific):
Literal files: CLANS.CFG, SERVERS.CFG, KONUNG.CFG, CLIENT.LOG, MAPS.RES,
SOUNDS_.RES, GRAPH.RES, LIGHTS.RES, OBJECTS.RES, GAME.RES, QUESTR.RES,
FLAMES.RES. Runtime-built: PLAY<w>R.RES, ME<w>R.RES, NEW<w>R.RES,
INTO<w>R.RES (w=640/800; pieces at 0x4402D0..0x44032B).
KONUNG.CFG is 124 bytes: 7 u32 (video mode 0x111/0x114 + 6 options) then
3 x 0x20 strings (player name 0x7570B0 used; 0x7570D0 and 0x757038 unresolved).
NOT the 28-byte single-player format. CLANS.CFG <=256 x 0x20 (0x72C26C);
SERVERS.CFG <=100 x 0x50 (0x6C899C).
MAPS.RES = 20 x 0x2F120 (3,856,000 exact). Record: terrain 0x6400;
collision 0xC800; 0x3E80 -> 0x750D2C (1000 x 0x10); 0x8CA0 -> 0x6DF55C
(1000 x 0x24); 0x3200 -> 0x722B10; 0xC800 -> 0x716310. Index (id-1)*0x2F120.
GAME.
RES = 365,509 bytes: 0x20000->0x5C772C; 0xA00->0x5E772C (80 x 0x20);
0x20D00->0x577FD8 (21 map slots x 100 x 0x40); SEEK_CUR 0x109A gap;
0x14000->0x6CA8DC (320 x 0x100); 0x2C2B->0x6E81FC (3 x 0xEB9).
SOUNDS_.RES: 8-byte hdr + 1000 x {off,size} at 8000; 57 clips to 0x754D00.
GRAPH.RES: palette 0x20000; 11 blocks + 0x1000 + 27 x 0x4D28 (3+24).
LIGHTS.RES: 0x1000 + 0xAC0 + 19 x 0x1C80 = 145,088 of 145,472 (384 trailing).
OBJECTS.RES: 8-byte hdr + 512 x {off,size} at 0x1008; 30 x 0x3228 at 0x66A8EC,
rest 0x100 at 0x73092C.
QUESTR.RES: 7 blocks 0x1F400,600,64000,12000,12000,120000,0x4B0.
FLAMES.RES: 74 frames, pointer table 0x754BD8.
Runtime observation: without ME640R.RES the client exits 1 and logs
"ME640R.RES not found rc=16".

# Konung dialogue / quest system — reverse-engineered notes

Target: `konung.res` (the shipped PE32/i386 game executable) + `QUEST_R.RES`.
All addresses are virtual addresses in `konung.res`; block addresses are the
fixed BSS buffers the loader writes into.

## 1. Which file holds dialogue

**`QUEST_R.RES`** (shipped here as `QUESTR.RES` / `Quest_r.res`, 337 800 bytes).
`konung.res` holds only the *code*. The loader `FUN_0043a870` (`0x43A870`) opens
`<base>\QUEST_R.RES` (string `0x450770`) right after `Game.N`, and reads its
payload as seven fixed, contiguous blocks whose sizes sum to the file size:

#### `drawer_konung_general_1de0eb2cb9750d77e60dd5ed`

- **wing:** konung
- **room:** general
- **source_file:** C:\Users\z10ba\AppData\Local\Temp\opencode\konung-docs\subsystems\DIALOG_SYSTEM.md
- **filed_at:** 2026-10-21T19:40:16.462497
- **added_by:** mempalace
- **hall:** creative
- **chunk_index:** 2
- **chunk_total:** 22

ine lists of B3 offsets |
| B7 | 336 600 | 1 200 | `0x615068` | `u32[300]` quest/global flag slots |

If the open fails the original reports `"File QUEST.RES not found"` (`0x45077C`).

## 2. Entry point — right-clicking an NPC

The gameplay driver `FUN_0042260C` (`0x42260C`) selects the clicked actor into
`_DAT_0081D578` and, on a **right-click** (right-button branch, `_DAT_0081D5B0 &
2`, command code 0) when that actor's `Entity+0xD0` (`dialogue_id`) is not
`0xFF`, calls the dialogue opener:

```c
if ((_DAT_0081D578 != 0) && (*(byte *)(_DAT_0081D578 + 0xD0) != 0xFF) &&
    (_DAT_0081D624 == 0) && ((_DAT_0081D56C[3] & 4) == 0)) {
    FUN_00435290(_DAT_0081D578);   // dialog entry
    FUN_0042CBD0(0);               // render dialog interface mode 0
}
```

`FUN_00435290(entity)`:

#### `drawer_konung_general_b1fe9c87d04c6a8f7e73d4b8`

`Game.N`, and reads its
payload as seven fixed, contiguous blocks whose sizes sum to the file size:

| Block | File offset | Length | Buffer VA | Role |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| B1 | 0 | 128 000 | `0x5C2D90` | node/option records, stride `0x10` |
| B2 | 128 000 | 600 | `0x5E2190` | `u16[300]`: `dialogue_id -> first node` |
| B3 | 128 600 | 64 000 | `0x5E23E8` | CP866 text blob (NUL-terminated) |
| B4 | 192 600 | 12 000 | `0x5F1DE8` | `u16[]` condition programs (`0xFFFF`-ended) |
| B5 | 204 600 | 12 000 | `0x5F4CC8` | `u16[]` action programs (`0xFFFF`-ended) |
| B6 | 216 600 | 120 000 | `0x5F7BA8` | `u16[]` per-line lists of B3 offsets |
| B7 | 336 600 | 1 200 | `0x615068` | `u32[300]` quest/global flag slots |

}
```

`FUN_00435290(entity)`:

```c
_DAT_0081D674 = *(int *)(&DAT_005E218E + entity->dialogue_id * 2) >> 16;  // B2[id] -> node
_DAT_0081D574 = entity;                                                   // NPC pointer
_DAT_00831C4C = _DAT_0081D674;
if ((entity->archetype & 0x40) == 0) { window = rng()%4 + 0x0C; }        // humanoid
else if (archetype == 'Q') window = 0x11;
else if (archetype == 'R') window = 0x12;
else if (archetype == 'S') window = 0x13;
else window = 0x10;
if (FUN_00434C74()) FUN_0042A13C(window, 0, 0, 0);                       // open window
```

So `dialogue_id` indexes **B2** for the first node, and the NPC archetype
(`Entity+0x04`) + resolution selects the window template id (`0x0C..0x13`).

e, and the NPC archetype
(`Entity+0x04`) + resolution selects the window template id (`0x0C..0x13`).

## 3. B1 record (`0x10` bytes)

Two shapes share the 16-byte slot (`FUN_00434C74`):

- **Page header** (`+0x00 = +0x02 = 0xFFFF`):
  `+0x04` next page, `+0x0A` `0xFFFF` = display page / `0xFFFE` = branch page,
  `+0x0C`(low) = *unconditional* entry count, `+0x0E`(high) = *conditional* entry
  count. The decompiler reads the entry count as `(u32 @ +0x0C) >> 16` for the
  first (conditional) pass and the low word for the second pass, so a **display
  page's entries** are the `conditional + unconditional` records starting at
  `header+1`; a **branch page's cases** are the `conditional` records starting at
  the header itself.

#### `drawer_konung_general_8a98f51dd4291be66c36e867`

- **wing:** konung
- **room:** general
- **source_file:** C:\Users\z10ba\AppData\Local\Temp\opencode\konung-docs\subsystems\DIALOG_SYSTEM.md
- **filed_at:** 2026-10-21T19:40:16.466831
- **added_by:** mempalace
- **hall:** identity
- **chunk_index:** 5
- **chunk_total:** 22

`header+1`; a **branch page's cases** are the `conditional` records starting at
  the header itself.
- **Entry / option**: `+0x00` u32 = **B6 line index** (`0xFFFFFFFF` = none),
  `+0x04` next node (`0xFFFF` = fall back to the page, `0xFFFE` = end dialogue),
  `+0x06` B4 condition program id (`0xFFFF` = unconditional), `+0x0A` B5 action
  program id (`0xFFFF` = none).

Selection is handled in `FUN_0042260C` case `0x65`: it runs the option's action
program (`FUN_00434BB8`), resolves the next node from the option's `+0x04`
(`-1` -> fall back, `-2` -> end), rebuilds the option lists (`FUN_00434C74`) and
redraws (`FUN_0042CBD0(0)`).

redraws (`FUN_0042CBD0(0)`).

**Verified against the shipped file:** `Game.1` entity 277 (Гилли) has
`dialogue_id = 140` -> node 3763 (branch) -> page 3765 whose first entry's B6
line 28556 composes to the Черный Бор elder greeting *"Приветствую тебя, о
Великий Князь! …"*.

## 4. Text (B3 / B6)

`FUN_00435188` composes a line from a **B6** list: each `u16` word is a **B3**
byte offset, terminated by `0xFFFF`; fragments are decoded as CP866. A single
space (the separator string at `0x450618`) is inserted before a following
fragment whose first byte is a letter (`> 0x40`), `-`, or digit (`0x30..0x39`),
matching the original exactly. `B3` text is therefore an offset pool, not one

#### `drawer_konung_general_1f558cf4280b29f0e529035d`

- **wing:** konung
- **room:** general
- **source_file:** C:\Users\z10ba\AppData\Local\Temp\opencode\konung-docs\subsystems\DIALOG_SYSTEM.md
- **filed_at:** 2026-10-21T19:40:16.468858
- **added_by:** mempalace
- **hall:** technical
- **chunk_index:** 7
- **chunk_total:** 22

digit (`0x30..0x39`),
matching the original exactly. `B3` text is therefore an offset pool, not one
string; word boundaries are recovered by the separator rule.

## 5. Programs (B4 / B5) and flags (B7)

A program is a `u16` list ended by `0xFFFF`. Each word: low 9 bits = operand,
`0x2000` = run original script opcode `FUN_0043159C(operand)`, `0x4000` =
condition group separator (used only by the condition evaluator
`FUN_00434FE8`), `0x8000` = set/negate the B7 flag slot `operand`.

#### `drawer_konung_general_4a18e2119e6bd7576f86aad7`

- **wing:** konung
- **room:** general
- **source_file:** C:\Users\z10ba\AppData\Local\Temp\opencode\konung-docs\subsystems\DIALOG_SYSTEM.md
- **filed_at:** 2026-10-21T19:40:16.469863
- **added_by:** mempalace
- **hall:** technical
- **chunk_index:** 8
- **chunk_total:** 22

d only by the condition evaluator
`FUN_00434FE8`), `0x8000` = set/negate the B7 flag slot `operand`.

- **Condition `FUN_00434FE8(id)`**: OR-of-ANDs over B4; a term is either a B7
  flag bit `0x80` or an opcode predicate; `0x8000` negates a term.
- **Action `FUN_00434BB8(id)`**: for each word, either set/clear B7 flag
  `operand` bit `0x80` (per `0x8000`) or call `FUN_0043159C(operand)`.
- **Opcode dispatcher `FUN_0043159C(op)`**: `op < 0x38` -> `FUN_00432F8C(op+1)`;
  `< 0x70` -> `FUN_00433FF8(op-0x37)`; `< 0xA6` -> `FUN_0043395C(op-0x6F)`;
  `< 0xDC` -> `FUN_004347D0(op-0xA5)` (spawns an entity/item, kind `0x0B`);
  `< 0x112` -> `FUN_00433A8C(op-0xDB)`; else a table at `0x465BD4`
  (**unresolved** — entries decode as small integers, not code pointers).

## 6. Interface (INTR) — scope 1

The dialogue interface is drawn by **`FUN_0042CBD0(param = 0)`** (switch case
`param+1 == 1`; it sets interface mode `_DAT_0081D610 = 1`). This is the same
`FUN_0042CBD0` dispatcher as the character screen (case 3) and the quest log
(case 2) / inventory (case 5); `FUN_0042CBD0(-1)` (case 0) restores the normal
HUD (`INTR[3]` + party portraits).

Case 1 composes the window from three INTR sprites at per-resolution table
positions (`_DAT_0081D644`: 0 = 640, 1 = 800):

## 7. Script opcodes `0x112..0x16E` (actions / conditions)

For `op >= 0x112`, `FUN_0043159C` does `CALL [op*4 + 0x465BD4]` — an **op-indexed**
function-pointer table; entries below `0x112` are unused filler, so the real table
begins at `0x46601C`. Shipped dialogues use `op 0x112..0x16E` (93 handlers),
catalogued with handler VA, kind (predicate/action) and effect in
[`web/src/script.rs`](web/src/script.rs) (const `OPS`); the full human-readable
reference (including the low-opcode families `0x00..0x111`) is
[`QUEST_OPCODES.md`](QUEST_OPCODES.md).

settlement requirement table `0x458A06`; `0x123/0x14C/0x162/0x16B` = spawn/
recruit/remove world actors.

an item. In the remake all opcodes (including trade) remain **no-ops**.

## 9. Remake integration

`web/src/quest.rs` decodes the database (`QuestData::parse`, `dialogue_start`,
`node`/`option`, `entries`, `line_text`, `condition_program`/`action_program`,
`flag_byte`). `web/src/dialog.rs` implements the interface:

  the dialog). Hover highlights; click selects and follows the option's `+0x04`.

# Konung 1 Remake — Reverse-Engineered Source of Truth

**Revision:** 4 — binary re-audit of `konung.res` (corrections, binary-only
gates, new §28/§29), 2026-10-15  
**Status:** canonical implementation contract for the remake. 

> Revision 4 re-verified every address this document cites directly against
> `konung.res` (PE32/i386, image base `0x400000`) and the `Konung.N`/`Game.N`
> raw bytes. Section-level verdicts, corrections and newly recovered
> binary-only systems are recorded inline and in §28; the VAs are
> `konung.res`-specific (see §29 for `CLIENT.EXE`). 

This file supersedes earlier coordinate/rendering claims in previous revisions of `KONUNG_SOURCE_OF_TRUTH.md`.

supersedes earlier coordinate/rendering claims in previous revisions of `KONUNG_SOURCE_OF_TRUTH.md`.

The rule is simple: **the original binaries are authoritative; the remake is not**. A visually plausible old implementation is evidence worth preserving, but it is not allowed to override a complete original-code trace. Conversely, a single disassembled arithmetic function is not enough to assign semantics until its callers and consumers are traced.

# 0. Evidence levels and implementation discipline

Use these labels literally.

1. **ORIGINAL-CODE VERIFIED** — directly observed in `konung.res` / `CLIENT.EXE` machine code, including the relevant caller/consumer chain.
2. **BINARY-LAYOUT VERIFIED** — offsets, strides and table boundaries close exactly against original files.
3. **DATA-CORRELATED** — values in real saves/resources strongly match the observed code behavior.
4. **REMAKE-REGRESSION VERIFIED** — empirically preserves a known-correct remake appearance/behavior, but is not necessarily original semantics.
5. **WORKING INTERPRETATION** — useful semantic name, not fully proven.
6. **UNKNOWN** — do not invent a meaning.

Before changing a subsystem, answer these checks:

## 0.1 Mandatory rule for agents

Before changing a subsystem, answer these checks:

```text
CHECK 1: Which coordinate/data domain is this field in?
CHECK 2: What exact original routine consumes it?
CHECK 3: Is there an explicit conversion before use?
CHECK 4: Is the current remake behavior original, compatibility-only, or simply wrong?
CHECK 5: Am I adding a scale/clamp/fallback that does not exist in the original?
```

If a fact is marked UNKNOWN and is not required for the current task, **preserve it and continue**. Do not stop implementation merely to ask how to rename an unrelated unknown field. Ask only when an unresolved point is a true blocker.

Do not do any of the following without original-code evidence:

## 0.2 Forbidden inference patterns

Do not do any of the following without original-code evidence:

```text
"the array has 80 columns, therefore world X must be 0..79"
"the visible surface ends here, therefore spawn coordinates outside it are invalid"
"this number looks like a sprite id, therefore use it as a sprite id"
"the old renderer divides by two, therefore the file coordinates must be doubled"
"one group produced one visible actor, therefore one Group == one Actor"
```

Do not add silent fallback humans/monsters, center-spawn fallbacks, coordinate clamps, `/2`, `/4`, or arbitrary bounds to make screenshots look plausible.

# 1. Correction history: coordinate/render pipeline

Several earlier claims were individually plausible but incomplete. This section exists so they are not resurrected later.

## 1.1 RETRACTED: "Group spawn coordinates → collision coordinates unresolved"

This is now **false**.

The original runtime placement routine around `konung.res:0x411DE4`:

1. obtains the owning Group through `Entity+0x05`;
2. generates `(x,y)` from Group rectangle A;
3. writes the generated values **directly** to `Entity+0x08/+0x0A`;
4. immediately checks collision using byte addressing equivalent to:

```text
collision_base + x * 0x140 + y * 2
```

Therefore:

```text
Group spawn coordinate
    == Entity x/y coordinate
    == collision-grid coordinate
```

#### `drawer_konung_general_05ee3f0d529be8cb39dcba7b`

- **wing:** konung
- **room:** general
- **source_file:** C:\Users\z10ba\AppData\Local\Temp\opencode\konung-docs\subsystems\KONUNG_SOURCE_OF_TRUTH.md
- **filed_at:** 2026-10-21T19:40:17.193089
- **added_by:** mempalace

- **chunk_total:** 93

e:

```text
Group spawn coordinate
    == Entity x/y coordinate
    == collision-grid coordinate
```

for this runtime path.

## 1.2 RETRACTED: "0x438A78 semantic role unresolved"

The transform is now traced into actor rendering.

Original code passes `Entity.x/+0x08` and `Entity.y/+0x0A` to `0x438A78`, stores the outputs in Entity runtime render fields (`+E8`, `+F0` in memory), and the actor renderer later consumes those values with the camera offsets.

Therefore `0x438A78` is the canonical Entity-grid → local-world-pixel transform.

## 1.3 RETRACTED: "old remake x*29,y*16 is the canonical actor projection"

The old remake placed the protagonist plausibly because the **base-terrain lattice was also rendered at half the original spacing**. Two wrong transforms partially cancelled visually.

The old mapping:

```text
local_x = 29 + x*29
local_y = y*16
```

is not the canonical original Entity projection.

## 1.4 Root cause of the visual regression

The remake corrected the actor projection without simultaneously correcting the terrain lattice. The actor moved to original-scale coordinates while the map remained half-scale.

The original uses:

#### `drawer_konung_general_98cf9f148a1e8c6703f1d7c8`

- **wing:** konung
- **room:** general
- **source_file:** C:\Users\z10ba\AppData\Local\Temp\opencode\konung-docs\subsystems\KONUNG_SOURCE_OF_TRUTH.md

- **chunk_index:** 8
- **chunk_total:** 93

The actor moved to original-scale coordinates while the map remained half-scale.

The original uses:

```text
base-terrain record lattice: 116 px horizontally, 32 px vertically, 58 px odd-row offset
runtime/entity lattice:       58 px horizontally, 16 px vertically
```

The relation is intentionally 2:1. The 80×160 base-terrain record count and the 320×160 runtime/collision grid are different resolutions of the same map geometry.

# 3. Runtime / Entity / Group-spawn / collision grid

**Status: ORIGINAL-CODE VERIFIED**

Bounds:

```text
x = 0..319   (320 values)
y = 0..159   (160 values)
```

The expanded collision table is `u16` and x-major:

```rust
index = x * 160 + y
```

Byte addressing in the original:

```text
collision_base + x * 0x140 + y * 2
0x140 = 160 * sizeof(u16)
```

A runtime cell is free for the placement path when:

```rust
(collision[x * 160 + y] & 0x0FFF) == 0
```

`x=268` is valid. Any statement that `268` is outside a 320-wide grid is simply an arithmetic error.

# 4. Entity coordinate → local-world-pixel projection

**Status: ORIGINAL-CODE VERIFIED**  
Original function: around `konung.res:0x438A78`.

Given integer runtime/entity cell `(x,y)`:

```rust
local_x = y * 58 + (2 - (x & 1)) * 29;
local_y = x * 16 + 16;
```

Equivalent:

```text
x even: local_x = y*58 + 58
x odd:  local_x = y*58 + 29
local_y = x*16 + 16
```

Known exact checks:

```text
Entity (0,0) -> (58,16)
Entity (1,0) -> (29,32)
Entity (2,0) -> (58,48)
Entity (5,7) -> (435,96)
```

## 4.1 Consumer chain

The original calls the transform with:

```text
Entity +08 = x
Entity +0A = y
```

#### `drawer_konung_general_a2daeda1fe743e7d4116f0fd`

- **wing:** konung
- **room:** general
- **source_file:** C:\Users\z10ba\AppData\Local\Temp\opencode\konung-docs\subsystems\KONUNG_SOURCE_OF_TRUTH.md
- **filed_at:** 2026-10-21T19:40:17.197216
- **added_by:** mempalace
- **hall:** memory
- **chunk_index:** 11
- **chunk_total:** 93

.1 Consumer chain

The original calls the transform with:

```text
Entity +08 = x
Entity +0A = y
```

and stores the results in runtime Entity rendering fields. Actor rendering later uses those precomputed pixel coordinates plus camera offsets and sprite-specific offsets.

This is not a heuristic and must not be replaced by `29+x*29, y*16` once the terrain renderer is corrected.

# 5. Camera / viewport transform

**Status: ORIGINAL-CODE VERIFIED at conceptual level**

The original keeps local-world-pixel coordinates separate from camera offsets. Render paths for terrain, GRAPH placements, props, and actors all add global camera/view offsets before final draw calls.

Conceptually:

#### `drawer_konung_general_00464967ccb737bd4dd923a9`

- **wing:** konung
- **room:** general
- **source_file:** C:\Users\z10ba\AppData\Local\Temp\opencode\konung-docs\subsystems\KONUNG_SOURCE_OF_TRUTH.md
- **filed_at:** 2026-10-21T19:40:17.198222
- **added_by:** mempalace
- **hall:** memory
- **chunk_index:** 12
- **chunk_total:** 93

ements, props, and actors all add global camera/view offsets before final draw calls.

Conceptually:

```rust
screen_x = local_world_x + camera_x + sprite_offset_x;
screen_y = local_world_y + camera_y + sprite_offset_y;
```

Relevant original globals used repeatedly by the renderer are around:

```text
0x81D5CC  horizontal camera/render offset
0x81D5D0  vertical camera/render offset
```

The exact higher-level camera-follow policy is not required for population restoration. The important contract is:

- file/grid coordinates are never mutated merely to follow the camera;
- projection produces local-world pixels;
- camera is applied afterward.

## 5.1 Surface-memory rule

The original is a viewport renderer. It does not require constructing a single giant pre-baked image of the whole map.

#### `drawer_konung_general_3a7a832508cc353eb5839872`

- **wing:** konung
- **room:** general

- **chunk_total:** 93

viewport renderer. It does not require constructing a single giant pre-baked image of the whole map.

A remake may cache a full surface if convenient, but **surface size is an implementation choice, not world bounds**. For fidelity and memory/performance, viewport culling is preferred after the coordinate pipeline is stable.

# 7. Base terrain layer (`Konung.N +0x00000`)

**Status: ORIGINAL-CODE VERIFIED renderer geometry; BINARY-LAYOUT VERIFIED storage**

Storage:

```text
12800 records × u16
160 rows × 80 records per row
```

## 7.1 Each u16 is two independent 1-byte terrain layers

The original terrain renderer reads **both bytes** of each 16-bit record separately.

For each byte:

```text
0     => no tile in that sublayer
n > 0 => draw terrain resource index (n - 1)
```

Therefore a canonical parser should expose something like:

```rust
struct BaseTerrainRecord {
    layer0: u8,
    layer1: u8,
}
```

#### `drawer_konung_general_6d9fd2f72e58bca942f0e6c7`

- **wing:** konung
- **room:** general
- **source_file:** C:\Users\z10ba\AppData\Local\Temp\opencode\konung-docs\subsystems\KONUNG_SOURCE_OF_TRUTH.md
- **filed_at:** 2026-10-21T19:40:17.201263
- **added_by:** mempalace
- **hall:** general
- **chunk_index:** 15
- **chunk_total:** 93

uld expose something like:

```rust
struct BaseTerrainRecord {
    layer0: u8,
    layer1: u8,
}
```

Do not render only `record & 0xFF`. The high byte is a second drawable terrain layer.

## 7.2 Original terrain lattice

The original render loop around `0x42532C` advances:

```text
column step                = 0x74 = 116 px
row step                   = 0x20 = 32 px
odd-row horizontal offset  = 0x3A = 58 px
```

Ignoring camera offset:

```rust
terrain_local_x = column * 116 + (row & 1) * 58;
terrain_local_y = row * 32;
```

This is the canonical base-terrain-record lattice.

### Important

Do **not** use the old remake lattice:

```text
column*58
row*16
parity*29
```

That is exactly half-scale.

## 7.3 Relationship to runtime grid

#### `drawer_konung_general_c4af5ac7ca474e1cd946f470`

- **chunk_total:** 93

ext
column*58
row*16
parity*29
```

That is exactly half-scale.

## 7.3 Relationship to runtime grid

The terrain record lattice has twice the step of the Entity/collision half-step lattice:

```text
terrain record step  = 116 × 32
runtime half-step    =  58 × 16
```

This is expected and explains why:

```text
80 × 160 terrain records = 12800 records
320 × 160 collision cells = 51200 cells
```

Do not attempt to force both tables into the same array dimensions.

# 8. Collision/passability source (`Konung.N +0x06400`)

**Status: ORIGINAL-CODE VERIFIED**

Storage:

```text
size  = 0xC800 = 51200 bytes
shape = 320 × 160 u8
index = x*160 + y
```

The location loader expands each byte `b` into runtime `u16` collision state:

#### `drawer_konung_general_1a71b07fcf14322083c357b0`

- **wing:** konung
- **room:** general
- **source_file:** C:\Users\z10ba\AppData\Local\Temp\opencode\konung-docs\subsystems\KONUNG_SOURCE_OF_TRUTH.md
- **filed_at:** 2026-10-21T19:40:17.203350
- **added_by:** mempalace
- **hall:** technical
- **chunk_index:** 17
- **chunk_total:** 93

index = x*160 + y
```

The location loader expands each byte `b` into runtime `u16` collision state:

```rust
if b == 0xFF {
    value = 0x0FFF;
} else if (b & 0x0F) == 0x0F {
    value = ((b as u16) << 8) | 0x0FFF;
} else {
    value = (b as u16) << 8;
}
```

Required decode checks:

```text
0x00 -> 0x0000
0x0F -> 0x0FFF
0x10 -> 0x1000
0x1F -> 0x1FFF
0xFF -> 0x0FFF
```

The low 12 bits are used as passability/occupancy state.

The table is also modified at runtime to incorporate dynamic occupancy/state. A pure Euclidean actor-separation rule is not the original system.

## 8.1 Runtime upper-bit semantics (ORIGINAL-CODE VERIFIED)

#### `drawer_konung_general_cf47b27d343d581263f32265`

- **wing:** konung
- **room:** general
- **source_file:** C:\Users\z10ba\AppData\Local\Temp\opencode\konung-docs\subsystems\KONUNG_SOURCE_OF_TRUTH.md
- **filed_at:** 2026-10-21T19:40:17.204376
- **added_by:** mempalace
- **hall:** creative
- **chunk_index:** 18
- **chunk_total:** 93

ration rule is not the original system.

## 8.1 Runtime upper-bit semantics (ORIGINAL-CODE VERIFIED)

`FUN_0043adf0` (Konung.N loader) and the actor setup/step code mark the upper
bits of the runtime `u16`. These are **not** terrain: passability still tests
only `& 0x0FFF`, but the markers carry real gameplay meaning.

```text
bit  0x1000 -> cell lies inside a Transition trigger rectangle
               (set at load for every cell of every transition whose +03
                source location == current location; see §13)
bit  0x2000 -> cell contains an active world-object / portal prop
               (set for each WorldObject at the current location)
```

#### `drawer_konung_general_5a73e62e77ba9ee123c4664b`

- **wing:** konung
- **room:** general
- **source_file:** C:\Users\z10ba\AppData\Local\Temp\opencode\konung-docs\subsystems\KONUNG_SOURCE_OF_TRUTH.md
- **filed_at:** 2026-10-21T19:40:17.205378
- **added_by:** mempalace
- **hall:** general
- **chunk_index:** 19
- **chunk_total:** 93

ive world-object / portal prop
               (set for each WorldObject at the current location)
```

Actor occupancy is merged into the same word: when an actor occupies a cell the
entity index is OR-ed into the `u16`, and the cell's low 12 bits are cleared
(`&= 0xF000`) when it leaves. This is why a blocked destination in
`FUN_0043da1f` can still be legal: that routine accepts a blocked goal only when
`(cell & 0x0FFF) == Entity+0x06` (the actor's own interaction target), otherwise
the route fails.

A remake that only reads `& 0x0FFF` (as the current `loc.rs`/`actors.rs` do) can
path correctly but silently ignores triggers, portals and actor occupancy. See
§28.

# 9. GRAPH placement layer (`Konung.N +0x12C00`)

**Status: ORIGINAL-CODE VERIFIED coordinate consumption; BINARY-LAYOUT VERIFIED storage**

```text
count  = 1000    (storage stride count)
stride = 0x10
```

The runtime loader loop (`FUN_0043adf0`) is bounded by `count < 999`, so at most
**999** records are active; the 1000th slot is never consumed. The sentinel that
ends the scan is "first dword has bit 31 set" (`< 0x80000000` unsigned), not only
the literal `0xFFFFFFFF`.

File-side record, relevant fields:

```text
+00 dword resource/tile index or sentinel 0xFFFFFFFF
+04 dword resource/palette metadata (patched by original loader)
+0C i16/u16 local pixel X anchor
+0E i16/u16 local pixel Y anchor
```

#### `drawer_konung_general_710e9805fad5303f26e4bb7b`

- **wing:** konung
- **room:** general
- **source_file:** C:\Users\z10ba\AppData\Local\Temp\opencode\konung-docs\subsystems\KONUNG_SOURCE_OF_TRUTH.md
- **filed_at:** 2026-10-21T19:40:17.207387
- **added_by:** mempalace
- **hall:** technical
- **chunk_index:** 21
- **chunk_total:** 93

a (patched by original loader)
+0C i16/u16 local pixel X anchor
+0E i16/u16 local pixel Y anchor
```

The original loader patches resource pointers/metadata but does **not** run the `+0C/+0E` coordinates through the Entity/grid projection.

The renderer directly uses:

```rust
screen_x = camera_x + graph_x_px;
screen_y = camera_y + graph_y_px;
```

Thus GRAPH placements are already in a **pixel-anchor domain**.

### Implementation rule

Do not treat GRAPH placement X/Y as terrain-record coordinates, runtime cells, or Entity coordinates. No `*58`, `*16`, `/2`, or parity transform is required.

```

## 10.1 Relevant file-side fields

```text
+00 i32  raw object resource index; negative terminates/marks unused region
+04 dword palette/resource state
...
+1E u16  raw grid Y-like coordinate
+20 u16  raw grid X-like coordinate

The loader (`FUN_0043adf0`) uses the raw index **directly** to address the
runtime OBJECTS object record (`raw*0x100 + 0x75FCB4`) and adds `30` (`0x1E`)
only to derive the **entity-type id** used by the 512-entry activity/occupancy
table `0x81B954` (`type = raw + 30`). The remake's `object_id = raw + 30`
therefore names the *type* index; confirm it is also the correct Objects.res
bank/sprite selector (the loader consumes the raw index for the object record
itself) before treating it as the render selector.

## 10.2 Canonical names for raw prop coordinates

Use:

```text
prop_grid_x = raw word +0x20
prop_grid_y = raw word +0x1E
```

#### `drawer_konung_general_b12e7a0405c5cb136a932cfe`

- **wing:** konung

- **chunk_total:** 93

or raw prop coordinates

Use:

```text
prop_grid_x = raw word +0x20
prop_grid_y = raw word +0x1E
```

Do not name `+1E` X merely because it appears first in memory.

## 10.3 Original prop grid → local-pixel conversion

The loader converts these raw grid coordinates in place:

```rust
prop_local_x = prop_grid_y * 58 - (prop_grid_x & 1) * 29;
prop_local_y = prop_grid_x * 16;
```

It stores the converted pixel anchors back into the runtime copy of the record.

This is **not** the base-terrain `116×32` lattice and is **not** `entity_to_local_px(y,x)`.

The relation to Entity projection is:

```text
entity_local_x(x,y) = prop_local_x(x,y) + 58
entity_local_y(x,y) = prop_local_y(x,y) + 16
```

#### `drawer_konung_general_2ce94a356af78a7bf5738b73`

- **added_by:** mempalace

- **chunk_index:** 25
- **chunk_total:** 93

``text
entity_local_x(x,y) = prop_local_x(x,y) + 58
entity_local_y(x,y) = prop_local_y(x,y) + 16
```

when the same runtime-grid `(x,y)` is used. This difference is an anchor convention, not a coordinate-domain mismatch.

## 10.4 Prop renderer consumption

The object renderer later takes the preconverted prop pixel anchor, adds camera offsets and frame/object-specific sprite offsets, then draws.

Conceptually:

```rust
screen_x = camera_x + prop_local_x + object_frame_offset_x;
screen_y = camera_y + prop_local_y + object_frame_offset_y;
```

### Answer to the previously-blocking "Prop projection" question

The canonical projection is:

```rust
fn prop_grid_to_local_px(x: i32, y: i32) -> (i32, i32) {
    (y * 58 - (x & 1) * 29, x * 16)
}
```

where `x = raw +0x20`, `y = raw +0x1E`.

- **added_by:** mempalace
- **hall:** memory
- **chunk_index:** 26
- **chunk_total:** 93

) -> (i32, i32) {
    (y * 58 - (x & 1) * 29, x * 16)
}
```

where `x = raw +0x20`, `y = raw +0x1E`.

Do **not** tie props to the 116×32 terrain-record lattice. Do **not** swap arguments into `entity_to_local_px`.

# 11. Unified map geometry summary

This table is the primary "do not ask again" reference.

| Source | Stored coordinate domain | Local-world-pixel transform before camera |
|---|---|---|
| Base terrain record `(col,row)` | 80×160 macro record lattice | `(col*116 + (row&1)*58, row*32)` |
| Collision cell `(x,y)` | runtime grid 320×160 | no direct draw; same grid domain as Entity/spawn |
| Group spawn `(x,y)` | runtime grid 320×160 | after assignment to Entity, use Entity transform |
| Entity `(x,y)` | runtime grid 320×160 | `(y*58 + (2-(x&1))*29, x*16+16)` |

- **chunk_total:** 93

use Entity transform |
| Entity `(x,y)` | runtime grid 320×160 | `(y*58 + (2-(x&1))*29, x*16+16)` |
| Prop raw `(x=+20,y=+1E)` | runtime half-step grid | `(y*58 - (x&1)*29, x*16)` |
| GRAPH placement `(+0C,+0E)` | local pixel anchor | identity |

Then, for draw:

```text
screen = local_world_pixel + camera_offset + sprite/frame offset
```

# 12. Original render pipeline and depth behavior

## 12.1 Background terrain

The original draws visible base-terrain records using the 116×32 macro lattice and both byte sublayers.

## 12.2 GRAPH placements

GRAPH pixel-anchor placements are drawn with camera offsets without grid conversion.

## 12.3 Props and actors are not conceptually one permanently baked background

#### `drawer_konung_general_31d21086052721b81a9d658d`

ail cases where an actor must pass behind a tall prop.

### Required long-term architecture

Prefer:

```text
static terrain/background
GRAPH background placements where appropriate
then dynamic depth queue containing:
    props that participate in occlusion
    actors/entities
    other runtime drawables
```

The exact original depth-key formula is not yet named in this document; preserve sprite bounds and projected Y information so it can be implemented without changing coordinate domains later.

## 12.4 Depth bucket — partial recovery (ORIGINAL-CODE VERIFIED)

`FUN_0042532c` (the world renderer) inserts actors into a **y-bucketed depth
queue**. The bucket index is the actor's screen bottom:

```c
bucket = (Entity+0xEC - camera_y) + (Entity+0xF4 >> 16);
```

#### `drawer_konung_general_9e56db841b367109dcf9fa1c`

- **wing:** konung

out grid conversion.

## 12.3 Props and actors are not conceptually one permanently baked background

Original code builds runtime draw/depth structures for object props and for Entities. Props carry animation/frame-dependent extents and actors carry projected render coordinates and sprite extents. This is evidence that depth/occlusion is dynamic.

Therefore a remake architecture that permanently bakes every tree/pillar/prop underneath every actor is not fully faithful. It may be temporarily acceptable for bring-up, but it will fail cases where an actor must pass behind a tall prop.

### Required long-term architecture

Prefer:

#### `drawer_konung_general_9b569e91cec124fee4985b88`

- **wing:** konung
- **room:** general
- **source_file:** C:\Users\z10ba\AppData\Local\Temp\opencode\konung-docs\subsystems\KONUNG_SOURCE_OF_TRUTH.md
- **filed_at:** 2026-10-21T19:40:17.215704
- **added_by:** mempalace
- **hall:** technical
- **chunk_index:** 30
- **chunk_total:** 93

dex is the actor's screen bottom:

```c
bucket = (Entity+0xEC - camera_y) + (Entity+0xF4 >> 16);
```

i.e. projected screen Y plus the sprite's half-height, where `+0xEC` is the
projected Y from §4/§16.1.5 and `+0xF4` (high word) is the sprite extent. The
queue head array is `0x825468` with a 16-byte slot per bucket and an overflow
chain at `0x81D768`; buckets are then drawn in ascending order up to
`_DAT_0082d178` (the max occupied bucket). This names the previously-unknown
depth key enough to reproduce the ordering; the *special* occlusion flags remain
open (§25 #5).

### 13.1.1 Runtime trigger marking (ORIGINAL-CODE VERIFIED)

At map load (`FUN_0043adf0`) every cell of every transition whose `+03` source
location equals the current location is marked in the runtime collision table:

```c
for (x = trig_x1(+09); x <= trig_x2(+0D); x++)
  for (y = trig_y1(+0B); y <= trig_y2(+0F); y++)
    collision_u16[x*160 + y] |= 0x1000;
```

The scan is bounded by `+00 == 0` (count computed by `FUN_0043a870`). This is the
mechanism behind the `0x1000` marker named in §8.1: gameplay trigger detection
keys off the marked cell, so it never needs the visual `244/245` gate prop.

`+05/+07` (arrival coordinate A/B) read as coordinates inside the `320×160`
runtime grid for real `Game.1` records (e.g. `(24,6)`, `(142,134)`), strongly
supporting the "arrival coordinate" role. The routine that *consumes* `+05/+07`
on arrival was not located: the only static reference to the transition table
base `0x7891b4` is inside the loader itself (`0x43be2e`). Treat the consumer as a
remaining unknown (§25).

## 13.2 Portal visual props are a separate concern

Visual gate/teleport OBJECT props and logical transition records do not need to be bound by nearest-pixel heuristics for core gameplay. The original trigger logic can operate directly from transition records.

### Canonical rule

For gameplay transition detection:

```text
use Transition trigger rectangle in Entity/runtime coordinates
```

Do not require a visual gate prop to be found first.

For rendering a gate prop, use the prop projection from section 10.

## 15.1 Group → Entity traversal

**ORIGINAL-CODE VERIFIED**

```rust
first = group.first_entity;
count = group.entity_count;
for id in first .. first + count {

}
```

Entity stride is `0x100`.

A Group is not a drawable actor.

Original location setup iterates group indices beginning at **1**, checks `Group+0x1E != 0`, checks location, and uses `+0x1C`/population state.

## 15.3 Spawn rectangle A

Initial runtime placement uses:

```text
x1 = +08
x2 = +0C
y1 = +10
y2 = +14
```

The Group record is interleaved. Do not decode `+08,+0A,+0C,+0E` as one rectangle.

Rectangle B is:

```text
x1 = +0A
x2 = +0E
y1 = +12
y2 = +16
```

Its exact long-form semantic name remains **UNKNOWN** until separately proven.

# 16. Original runtime placement algorithm

**Status: ORIGINAL-CODE VERIFIED**

Routine around `0x411DE4`.

For rectangle A:

#### `drawer_konung_general_36be62e8199995ff76656dfd`

- **wing:** konung
- **room:** general
- **source_file:** C:\Users\z10ba\AppData\Local\Temp\opencode\konung-docs\subsystems\KONUNG_SOURCE_OF_TRUTH.md
- **filed_at:** 2026-10-21T19:40:17.224909
- **added_by:** mempalace
- **hall:** technical
- **chunk_index:** 38
- **chunk_total:** 93

lacement algorithm

**Status: ORIGINAL-CODE VERIFIED**

Routine around `0x411DE4`.

For rectangle A:

```text
half_x   = trunc_toward_zero((x2-x1)/2)
half_y   = trunc_toward_zero((y2-y1)/2)
center_x = trunc_toward_zero((x1+x2+1)/2)
center_y = trunc_toward_zero((y1+y2+1)/2)
```

Up to 100 attempts:

```rust
if half_x < 2 {
    dx = 0;
} else {
    dx = rand() % half_x;
    if rand()%2 == 0 { dx = -dx; }
}

if half_y < 2 {
    dy = 0;
} else {
    dy = rand() % half_y;
    if rand()%2 == 0 { dy = -dy; }
}

x = center_x + dx;
y = center_y + dy;
```

The candidate is written directly to `Entity.x/y` and accepted only if:

```rust
0 <= x < 320
0 <= y < 160
(collision[x*160+y] & 0x0FFF) == 0
```

If all 100 attempts fail, the routine returns failure. **No center fallback.**

#### `drawer_konung_general_1bbcb210c074aa5f2e2ccb9c`

- **wing:** konung
- **room:** general
- **source_file:** C:\Users\z10ba\AppData\Local\Temp\opencode\konung-docs\subsystems\KONUNG_SOURCE_OF_TRUTH.md
- **filed_at:** 2026-10-21T19:40:17.225949
- **added_by:** mempalace
- **hall:** technical
- **chunk_index:** 39
- **chunk_total:** 93

] & 0x0FFF) == 0
```

If all 100 attempts fail, the routine returns failure. **No center fallback.**

The original caller may shorten the group's active count when placement fails; do not silently pretend the actor was placed.

Exact failure mechanism (ORIGINAL-CODE VERIFIED, `FUN_0043adf0`): on the first
failed placement (`FUN_00411de4` returns 0) the loader writes the **ordinal of
the failing entity** into `Group+0x1C` and stops placing that group.

Bounds note: `FUN_00411de4` itself tests **only** the collision cell
(`(collision[x*160+y] & 0x0FFF) == 0`). There is no explicit
`0 <= x < 320` / `0 <= y < 160` test inside the routine; in-range candidates are
guaranteed by the rectangle it is handed. The bounds check listed above is
therefore defensive, not a literal original instruction.

# 16.1 Original pathfinding and movement

**Status: ORIGINAL-CODE VERIFIED** (`konung.res`; addresses are VAs in the
x86 PE, image base `0x00400000`).

Movement is a **direction-code queue**, not a list of cells and not a
screen-vector `atan2` facing.

## 16.1.1 Issuing a walk command — `FUN_004142D4(entity, tx, ty, mode)`

#### `drawer_konung_general_204bfef5105df17e22fa1294`

- **wing:** konung
- **room:** general
- **source_file:** C:\Users\z10ba\AppData\Local\Temp\opencode\konung-docs\subsystems\KONUNG_SOURCE_OF_TRUTH.md
- **filed_at:** 2026-10-21T19:40:17.227955
- **added_by:** mempalace
- **hall:** creative
- **chunk_index:** 41
- **chunk_total:** 93

reen-vector `atan2` facing.

## 16.1.1 Issuing a walk command — `FUN_004142D4(entity, tx, ty, mode)`

```c
entity + 0x0C/u16 = tx;          // target_x
entity + 0x0E/u16 = ty;          // target_y
entity + 0x02/u8  = mode;        // flags_a (0x48 for wander)
entity + 0x14/u8  = 0xFF;        // clear queue terminator
if (FUN_0043DA1F(entity) == 0) return 0;   // no route -> command fails
entity[2] &= ~0x40;
FUN_0041435C(entity, (entity[3] & 4) ? 1 : 7);  // walk, battle/peaceful
return 1;
```

The target fields are written **before** pathfinding, so a failed command still
records the destination but does not start moving. A blocked or unreachable
point therefore never moves the actor ("moving starts from a click on an
acceptable point").

## 16.1.2 Route builder — `FUN_0043DA1F(entity)`

#### `drawer_konung_general_094f3c60c44a35cd88f77b59`

- **added_by:** mempalace
- **hall:** memory
- **chunk_index:** 42
- **chunk_total:** 93

ving starts from a click on an
acceptable point").

## 16.1.2 Route builder — `FUN_0043DA1F(entity)`

A weighted grid search over the 320×160 collision table
(`DAT_004EE6B0`, `index = x*160 + y`, low-12 free). It writes the direction used
to *enter* each visited cell and finally stores the forward direction list in
`entity + 0x13` (32 bytes, terminator `-1` at `+0x32`), i.e. **at most 31
steps**.

If the destination cell is blocked (low-12 non-zero), the routine aborts and
returns 0 (except the entity's own interaction target).

## 16.1.3 The 8 movement directions are parity-dependent — `FUN_0043D922`

Neighbour deltas come from two 8-entry `{i16 dx, i16 dy}` tables selected by the
parity of the current cell's `x` (the staggered isometric lattice), read
verbatim:

#### `drawer_konung_general_eaf6a2d3e82c0dd07ee3b579`

- **wing:** konung
- **room:** general
- **source_file:** C:\Users\z10ba\AppData\Local\Temp\opencode\konung-docs\subsystems\KONUNG_SOURCE_OF_TRUTH.md
- **filed_at:** 2026-10-21T19:40:17.230287
- **added_by:** mempalace
- **hall:** general
- **chunk_index:** 43
- **chunk_total:** 93

s selected by the
parity of the current cell's `x` (the staggered isometric lattice), read
verbatim:

```text
x even (0x480110): 0:(0,-1) 1:(-1,0)  2:(-2,0) 3:(-1,1) 4:(0,1) 5:(1,1) 6:(2,0) 7:(1,0)
x odd  (0x48010C): 0:(0,-1) 1:(-1,-1) 2:(-2,0) 3:(-1,0) 4:(0,1) 5:(1,0) 6:(2,0) 7:(1,-1)
```

`FUN_0043D922(out_x, out_y, x, y, dir)` writes `(x+dx, y+dy)` and validates
`0..320 × 0..160`. Edge weights in the search are 10 for dirs `1/3/5/7` and 14
for dirs `0/2/4/6`. Ported as `coords.rs::DIR_EVEN`/`DIR_ODD`,
`coords.rs::step_by_direction`, `coords.rs::neighbors`,
`coords.rs::direction_cost` and `actors.rs::find_path`.

## 16.1.4 Advancing the queue — `FUN_004123BC`, `FUN_0041435C`, `FUN_00413DE4`

Each logic frame advances the animation (`FUN_00413DE4`). A walking entity steps
**one cell per completed walk cycle** and only when
`Entity+0x01 (facing) == Entity+0x13 (queue head)` (`FUN_004123BC`); after the
step the queue is shifted left (`FUN_0043EA79(+0x13, +0x14, 0x1F)`). Because the
facing must equal the pending direction, a straight walk keeps a **constant
facing** — the remake's old BFS-cell + `atan2` facing produced the zigzag.

#### `drawer_konung_general_8b01c56e4363db8c40dd8557`

- **wing:** konung
- **room:** general
- **source_file:** C:\Users\z10ba\AppData\Local\Temp\opencode\konung-docs\subsystems\KONUNG_SOURCE_OF_TRUTH.md
- **filed_at:** 2026-10-21T19:40:17.232297
- **added_by:** mempalace
- **hall:** memory
- **chunk_index:** 45
- **chunk_total:** 93

t walk keeps a **constant
facing** — the remake's old BFS-cell + `atan2` facing produced the zigzag.

**Commit timing (verified):** the actual cell step is performed by the *tail* of
`FUN_0041435C`, which runs whenever the action is walk (`5`/`1`/`7` reaching that
path) and the queue head `+0x13 != 0xFF`: it calls `FUN_0043D922` from
`+0x08/+0x0A` and writes the resulting neighbor back into `+0x08/+0x0A`.
`FUN_0041435C` is invoked (a) immediately after a route is built and (b) after
each `FUN_004123BC` pop, so **each step is committed at the START of its walk
segment**. Because `FUN_0041435C` first stores the pre-step projection into
`+0xE8/+0xF0` (see §16.1.5) and draws with the `+0xE0` frame dx/dy anchor, the
stored cell leads the sprite by exactly one step during the cycle. A mid-walk

#### `drawer_konung_general_91bca2870506a1e73862a67c`

8`

`FUN_00438A78(out_x, out_y, x, y)`:

```text
*out_x = y*58 + (2-(x&1))*29
*out_y = x*16 + 16
```

`FUN_0041435C` calls it with `Entity+0x08/+0x0A`, stores the result into the
runtime render fields `Entity+0xE8/+0xF0`, and derives screen coordinates
`Entity+0xE4 = camera_x + +0xE8 + frame_dx`,
`Entity+0xEC = camera_y + +0xF0 + frame_dy`. It **is** therefore the actor render
projection (see §4); unlike the separate prop (§10.3) and terrain (§7.2)
lattices. Ported as `coords.rs::entity_to_local_px`.

## 17.1 Runtime render fields in memory

Original setup computes projected pixel coordinates from Entity x/y and stores them in runtime fields used by the renderer (notably around `+E8/+F0`). These are derived state and should not replace persisted grid x/y in the remake model.

# 18. Entity visual selection

**Status: ORIGINAL-CODE VERIFIED**

Renderer family selection is based on:

#### `drawer_konung_general_7704d50afd4da068dc4c9a02`

- **wing:** konung
- **room:** general
- **source_file:** C:\Users\z10ba\AppData\Local\Temp\opencode\konung-docs\subsystems\KONUNG_SOURCE_OF_TRUTH.md
- **filed_at:** 2026-10-21T19:40:17.237497
- **added_by:** mempalace
- **hall:** technical
- **chunk_index:** 51
- **chunk_total:** 93

Entity visual selection

**Status: ORIGINAL-CODE VERIFIED**

Renderer family selection is based on:

```rust
entity.archetype & 0x40
```

not `kind == 6`.

```text
bit clear -> humanoid/base graphics family, block size 0x4D28
bit set   -> object/monster/special family, block size 0x3228
```

`Entity+D6` is a family-local visual index.

`Entity+D8` is palette/palette-base data, not a sprite-template id.

Canonical names:

```rust
visual_index: u8;      // D6
palette_offset: u32;   // D8
palette_id = palette_offset / 512; // current resource-layout convention
```

Do not fall back to "any matching human/monster" when resolution fails. Return an explicit error.
