# 19. ItemInstance[8192], stride 0x10

```text
+00 u8 kind/category; 0..5 map to equipment slot index
+01 i8 modifier_id; -1 none
+02 i8 special_id;  -1 none
+03 u8 prototype_id
+04 u32 union value
+08 u32 union max/secondary value
+0C u16 poison
+0E u16 flags
```

Durable equipment commonly interprets:

```text
+04 f32 current durability
+08 f32 max durability
```

Stackables/ammunition use:

```text
+04 u32 quantity
```

Known flag:

```text
+0E bit0 -> unidentified/unknown presentation path
```

**Instance-table source (ORIGINAL-CODE VERIFIED).** The table that resolves a
loaded game's inventory/equipment ids is the *loaded save file* (`Game.0/1/2`)
at offset `0` (8192 × 16, §14). The original loader `FUN_0043a870` (string
`GAME.` @ `0x450747`) reads `0x20000` bytes of it into memory. The separate
`Game.res` file is **not** this table for a loaded game (it supplies the variant
table read by the renderer); resolving save ids against `Game.res` yields wrong
items. Class = `+00`, subtype = `+03` (the selector `FUN_0042ba40` reads
`*param >> 0x18` as subtype and the low byte as class).

# 20. ItemPrototype, stride 0x40

Prototype table resides in original executable/resource data.

```text
+00..+2B name[44], CP866, NUL terminated
+2C u16 combat_value
+2E u16 durability
+30 u16 requirement_stat
+32 u16 requirement_value
+34 u16 range/reach
+36 u16 price
+38 u16 weight_grams
+3A u16 inventory_icon_id
+3C u16 world_sprite_id
+3E i16 equipped_visual_id / overlay set; -1 none
```

**Table bases (ORIGINAL-CODE VERIFIED).** The class selects a table
(`FUN_0042ba40` / `FUN_0042f5f8`). The icon/weight fields are addressed from the
selector base (`icon = u16@+0x02`, `weight = u16@+0x00`); the CP866 name lives in
a **parallel table with a different base**, read at `+0x00`:

#### `drawer_konung_general_9b582945546eb3ddd97ab7ac`

- **wing:** konung
- **room:** general
- **source_file:** C:\Users\z10ba\AppData\Local\Temp\opencode\konung-docs\subsystems\KONUNG_SOURCE_OF_TRUTH.md
- **filed_at:** 2026-10-21T19:40:17.241580
- **added_by:** mempalace
- **hall:** identity
- **chunk_index:** 55
- **chunk_total:** 93

= u16@+0x00`); the CP866 name lives in
a **parallel table with a different base**, read at `+0x00`:

```text
class 0..=5 or 12 -> icon/weight 0x459B88   name 0x459B50   stride  64
class 6..=8       -> icon/weight 0x45812A   name 0x4580B0   stride 128
class 9           -> icon/weight 0x4594CA   name 0x459450   stride 128
class 10..=11     -> icon/weight 0x460996   name 0x46091C   stride 128
```

For the stride-`0x40` table the field offsets above (`+0x00` name … `+0x3E`
equipped-visual) hold exactly. The stride-`0x80` tables use the same `name@+00`
and `icon@+0x02` (relative to their selector base), but the remaining field
offsets differ and are **not** yet fully named. Do **not** read the name at
`icon_base + 8` / `+ 6`; that lands one record late (`+0x40` / `+0x80`).

Do **not** read the name at
`icon_base + 8` / `+ 6`; that lands one record late (`+0x40` / `+0x80`).

**Class 10 caveat (ORIGINAL-CODE VERIFIED).** The *selector* `FUN_0042ba40`
groups classes `10..=11` on base `0x460996`, but the *name/detail* routine
`FUN_0042f5f8` has **no `case 10`**: it falls through to its empty `default`, so
only class `11` gets a name there. Treat class `10` as icon/weight-only until a
name path is found.

`equipped_visual_id` (`+0x3E`) is the actor-overlay selector: it indexes the 24
GRAPH human-overlay blocks (`0..=23`, `-1` = none). A Game.N entity's worn items
are the six `u16` ids at `Entity+0x66` (slot index `0..=5`; class/subtype come
from the item instance table). `FUN_00427e9c` composites them onto the humanoid
base frame:

#### `drawer_konung_general_1c8116cfbdffcb72eedf3339`

- **wing:** konung

ype come
from the item instance table). `FUN_00427e9c` composites them onto the humanoid
base frame:

```text
peaceful (Entity+0x03 & 4 == 0):
    order = DGROUP 0x4631FC[facing][6]          (slot order)
    no selector variant

battle (Entity+0x03 & 4 != 0):
    bucket = (action 1 -> 3, action 5 -> 2, action 4 -> 1, else 0)
    if Entity+0x12 == 1 and bucket != 2:
        order = DGROUP 0x462EFC[bucket][facing][6]
        variant slot = 1
    else:
        order = DGROUP 0x462BFC[bucket][facing][6]
        variant slots = { Entity+0x12 (unless 1), 5 }
```

For each slot in the order, the item's `equipped_visual_id` is read from the
stride-`0x40` prototype table by subtype; if the slot is a variant slot the
selector is advanced by one (the alternate "in hand" GRAPH block) before the
overlay is drawn. This is the port's `res.rs::overlay_plan` /
`compose_human` (`HUMAN_DRAW_ORDER_PEACE`, `HUMAN_DRAW_ORDER_BATTLE_A/B`).

The character-sheet paper doll is `FUN_0042ea6c`: the same base+overlay frame is
rendered with the base sprite's top-left at `(0x3C,0x14)` inside a 160x160
region and blitted at `DGROUP 0x46035C`/`0x460360` (640 -> `(470,127)`,
800 -> `(570,160)`).

# 21. Canonical implementation contract by render layer

An implementation agent should not need to ask which projection to use. Use this table.

## 21.1 Base terrain

```rust
for row in visible_rows {
    for col in visible_cols {
        let (lx,ly) = (col*116 + (row&1)*58, row*32);
        draw layer0 if nonzero, resource id layer0-1;
        draw layer1 if nonzero, resource id layer1-1;
    }
}
```

Apply camera after computing local coordinates.

## 21.2 GRAPH placements

```rust
local_x = record.x_px; // +0C
local_y = record.y_px; // +0E
```

No grid projection. Apply camera and sprite offsets.

## 21.3 Props

Parse raw:

```rust
grid_x = u16(+0x20);
grid_y = u16(+0x1E);
```

#### `drawer_konung_general_39cd2a830e3cf4f181ab03f8`

- **wing:** konung
- **room:** general
- **source_file:** C:\Users\z10ba\AppData\Local\Temp\opencode\konung-docs\subsystems\KONUNG_SOURCE_OF_TRUTH.md
- **filed_at:** 2026-10-21T19:40:17.246762
- **added_by:** mempalace
- **hall:** memory
- **chunk_index:** 61
- **chunk_total:** 93

nd sprite offsets.

## 21.3 Props

Parse raw:

```rust
grid_x = u16(+0x20);
grid_y = u16(+0x1E);
```

Project:

```rust
local_x = grid_y*58 - (grid_x&1)*29;
local_y = grid_x*16;
```

Then camera + object/frame offsets.

## 21.4 Entities, including protagonist and mobs

```rust
local_x = y*58 + (2-(x&1))*29;
local_y = x*16 + 16;
```

Then camera + actor/frame offsets.

## 21.5 Runtime-spawned `(0,0)` entities

Generate `(x,y)` from Group rect A, validate with `collision[x*160+y]`, store as runtime actor position, then use the **same Entity projection** as persisted actors.

No provisional accept-all validator remains in canonical mode.

## 21.6 Logical transitions

Detect against Transition trigger rectangle in Entity/runtime grid coordinates. Rendering a visual gate prop is independent.

# 22. Known current-remake deviations to fix before declaring C2 complete

The following are known deviations if still present in the working tree:

1. **Half-scale base terrain:** `58×16` / `29` record lattice instead of `116×32` / `58`.
2. **Only low byte of terrain u16 rendered:** high-byte second terrain layer omitted.
3. **Old actor/transition projection:** `29+x*29,y*16` instead of Entity projection.
4. **Prop projection tied to terrain lattice:** wrong. Use section 10.
5. **C2 accept-all placement validation:** wrong. Use collision table directly.
6. **Props permanently baked below all actors:** acceptable only as temporary bring-up; not faithful occlusion.

# 23. Required regression/unit tests

## 23.1 Entity projection

```text
(0,0) -> (58,16)
(1,0) -> (29,32)
(2,0) -> (58,48)
(5,7) -> (435,96)
```

## 23.2 Prop projection

```text
(x=0,y=0) -> (0,0)
(x=1,y=0) -> (-29,16)
(x=2,y=0) -> (0,32)
(x=5,y=7) -> (377,80)   // 7*58 -29, 5*16
```

## 23.3 Terrain lattice

Ignoring camera:

```text
terrain (0,0) -> (0,0)
terrain (1,0) -> (116,0)
terrain (0,1) -> (58,32)
terrain (1,1) -> (174,32)
```

## 23.4 Collision

#### `drawer_konung_general_a48009d074bf4c3a4376b4a3`

- **wing:** konung
- **room:** general
- **source_file:** C:\Users\z10ba\AppData\Local\Temp\opencode\konung-docs\subsystems\KONUNG_SOURCE_OF_TRUTH.md
- **filed_at:** 2026-10-21T19:40:17.251040
- **added_by:** mempalace
- **hall:** creative
- **chunk_index:** 64
- **chunk_total:** 93

)
terrain (1,0) -> (116,0)
terrain (0,1) -> (58,32)
terrain (1,1) -> (174,32)
```

## 23.4 Collision

```text
index(0,0)     = 0
index(0,159)   = 159
index(1,0)     = 160
index(319,159) = 51199
```

Must explicitly test X values >159, e.g. 268.

## 23.5 Terrain record dual layers

For a synthetic u16 record `0x0201`, the renderer must attempt to draw resource indices:

```text
layer0 byte 1 -> resource 0
layer1 byte 2 -> resource 1
```

not one tile id `1` or `513`.

24. Milestone order after this spec revision

Do not restart a large mixed task. Use narrow phases.

```text
R1  Correct base-terrain lattice to 116×32/58 and render both terrain bytes.
R2  Correct Entity/protagonist projection to the original transform simultaneously.
R3  Correct prop raw-grid projection; leave GRAPH placements as direct pixels.
R4  Restore C2 collision validation (x*160+y), remove provisional accept-all.
R5  Visual regression: protagonist, C1 persisted actors, C2 runtime actors, props all align.
R6  Improve prop/actor depth interleaving if needed.
R7  Population growth beyond current entity_count.
R8  Stats.
R9  Inventory/equipment UI.
```

Do not implement R2 without R1; that exact partial change caused the previous visual regression.

# 26. "Agent should not ask" decision matrix

When implementing map/population rendering:

```text
Question: Which terrain spacing?
Answer: 116×32, odd row +58.

Question: One or two bytes per terrain record?
Answer: two independent 1-based drawable byte layers.

Question: Which Entity projection?
Answer: x_screen_local = y*58 + (2-(x&1))*29; y_screen_local=x*16+16.

Question: Human vs object/monster renderer family?
Answer: Entity.archetype & 0x40.

Question: D6 / D8?
Answer: D6 visual index; D8 palette offset/base, not sprite template.

Question: Portal gameplay detection tied to visual prop?
Answer: no; logical transition trigger rectangle is sufficient.

Question: Allocate a giant full-map surface?
Answer: not required. Original is viewport-based; surface size is not world bounds.
```

---

# 27. Final invariant

Every coordinate-bearing record must have its domain named in the type or variable.

Bad:

```rust
let x = record.x;
let y = record.y;
```

Preferred:

```rust
let entity_cell_x = entity.x;
let prop_grid_x = prop.raw_x20;
let graph_pixel_x = graph.x_px;
let terrain_col = col;
```

tion: Which Entity projection?
Answer: x_screen_local = y*58 + (2-(x&1))*29; y_screen_local=x*16+16.

Question: Which prop projection?
Answer: raw x=+20, raw y=+1E; local=(y*58-(x&1)*29, x*16).

Question: Which GRAPH placement projection?
Answer: none; +0C/+0E are already pixel anchors.

Question: Group spawn coords same as Entity/collision coords?
Answer: yes, original placement writes them directly to Entity x/y and immediately indexes collision.

Question: Bounds for runtime grid?
Answer: x 0..319, y 0..159.

Question: Placement validation?
Answer: low 12 collision bits must be zero; max 100 attempts; no center fallback.

Answer: Entity.archetype & 0x40.

# 28. Binary-only systems present in `konung.res` but not in the remake

Revision 4 recovered the following original runtime mechanisms. They are
ORIGINAL-CODE VERIFIED in `konung.res` (unless marked otherwise) and are **not**
implemented in `web/src` at the time of writing. Treat each as a documented
feature gap, not a question.

## 28.1 Population growth (`FUN_00420a8c`)

#### `drawer_konung_general_462bbddeaa0fc5b9331f364b`

- **wing:** konung
- **room:** general
- **source_file:** C:\Users\z10ba\AppData\Local\Temp\opencode\konung-docs\subsystems\KONUNG_SOURCE_OF_TRUTH.md
- **filed_at:** 2026-10-21T19:40:17.258781
- **added_by:** mempalace
- **hall:** general
- **chunk_index:** 71
- **chunk_total:** 93

Treat each as a documented
feature gap, not a question.

## 28.1 Population growth (`FUN_00420a8c`)

Gated on: `Group+0x1E & 0x40` set (active), the group's location (`+0x1B`)
matching the current location, and `Group+0x1E - 0x41 < 0x10` (i.e. `+0x1E` in
`0x41..=0x50`). Growth then runs while `Group+0x1C < Group+0x18` and the periodic
population pass keys off `_DAT_0081d640 & 0xF`. (Also summarized in
`population.rs`; the remake never grows entities.)

## 28.2 Actor occupancy and the interaction exception

Actors OR their entity index into the collision `u16` and clear it on leaving
(§8.1). `FUN_0043da1f` accepts a blocked destination only when
`(cell & 0x0FFF) == Entity+0x06`. The remake's pathfinder ignores this exception.

## 28.3 Entity-type activity table `0x81B954`

#### `drawer_konung_general_8d40109be44d3571e9da1a4d`

- **wing:** konung
- **room:** general
- **source_file:** C:\Users\z10ba\AppData\Local\Temp\opencode\konung-docs\subsystems\KONUNG_SOURCE_OF_TRUTH.md
- **filed_at:** 2026-10-21T19:40:17.259814
- **added_by:** mempalace
- **hall:** family
- **chunk_index:** 72
- **chunk_total:** 93

x06`. The remake's pathfinder ignores this exception.

## 28.3 Entity-type activity table `0x81B954`

A 512-entry (`0x200`) table of `i32` states (`-1` disabled / `0` free /
positive active), reset per map load and incremented when actors of a type are
placed (`FUN_0043adf0`). It gates which entity types are active on a map. The
remake has no equivalent.

## 28.4 Group activation / traversal gate

Beyond `first_entity + entity_count` (§15.1), the loader and population pass
gate on `Group+0x1E != 0`, `Group+0x1B == location`, and the count comparison
`Group+0x1C < Group+0x18`. Population growth is not implemented (§28.1).

## 28.5 NPC / monster action machine (`FUN_004123bc`)

A per-entity action state machine driven by `Entity+0x00` stance tokens
(`M`/`O`/`L`/`I` …) with transitions through `FUN_00411fd0`, `FUN_00414078`,
`FUN_004116cc`, `FUN_00412e00`, and death/attack setup in `FUN_0041435c`. The
remake implements only idle/walk.

Two recovered details used by the remake:

#### `drawer_konung_general_0a08db96c6e3c7bef13ba55b`

- **wing:** konung
- **room:** general
- **source_file:** C:\Users\z10ba\AppData\Local\Temp\opencode\konung-docs\subsystems\KONUNG_SOURCE_OF_TRUTH.md
- **filed_at:** 2026-10-21T19:40:17.261818
- **added_by:** mempalace
- **hall:** general
- **chunk_index:** 74
- **chunk_total:** 93

in `FUN_0041435c`. The
remake implements only idle/walk.

Two recovered details used by the remake:

- **Idle is held, not looped for `flags_a & 0x40` entities.** The animation
  switch is entered directly only when `(Entity+0x02 & 0x40) == 0` (or action
  `2`/`3`/`9`); a `0x40` entity is routed through `FUN_004116cc` (0x4116CC:
  `[0x81d5f0] < 3 && entity > [0x81d568]`) into `FUN_00412e00`, which returns `0`
  while idle, so its idle cases `0`/`6` are not reached and the frame is held.
  Every entity in the shipped active groups is `flags_a = 0x40`.
- **Object/monster per-frame anchor table.** `FUN_00414078` (object branch
  `0x41410a`-`0x414143`) computes gx/gy at
  `block + 0x1728 + row*0x480 + direction*0x90 + fi*8` (gx at `+4`, gy at `+6`),

#### `drawer_konung_general_0f1ea688da1baaab74ae71e3`

- **wing:** konung
- **room:** general
- **source_file:** C:\Users\z10ba\AppData\Local\Temp\opencode\konung-docs\subsystems\KONUNG_SOURCE_OF_TRUTH.md
- **filed_at:** 2026-10-21T19:40:17.262826
- **added_by:** mempalace
- **hall:** identity
- **chunk_index:** 75
- **chunk_total:** 93

) computes gx/gy at
  `block + 0x1728 + row*0x480 + direction*0x90 + fi*8` (gx at `+4`, gy at `+6`),
  i.e. indexed by `(row, direction, frame-index)`, **not** by the animation frame
  id. The sprite itself is `block + 0x728 + frame_id*8`. The human GRAPH anchor
  is the analogous `block + 0x2F28 + action*0x300 + direction*0x60 + fi*8`.
- **AI selection is by group class, not entity flags (`FUN_00421b50`,
  0x421B50).** For each active group whose `Group+0x1B == current location`:
  `class(+0x1E) & 0x40` → `FUN_004123bc` (AI = `FUN_00412e00`); `class & 0x40 ==
  0 && class & 0x80` → `FUN_00415fc8` (AI = `FUN_004168a4`, waypoint/wander);
  both bits clear → inline AI `FUN_0041ecc8`. `FUN_00412e00` with
  `Group+0x1D == 0` only normalizes the action to idle `0`/`6` and returns

#### `drawer_konung_general_7795e6be6df18f388b686ef7`

- **wing:** konung

cc8`. `FUN_00412e00` with
  `Group+0x1D == 0` only normalizes the action to idle `0`/`6` and returns
  **without moving**, so **object/enemy groups (e.g. the giant ants, class
  `0x41`) idle in place**; only citizen groups (class `0x80`/`0x81`/`0x82`) run
  the waypoint/wander AI. `FUN_0043adf0(mode)` (mode 0 = new game / map entry;
  callers `FUN_00436214`) clears `Group+0x1D` for `class & 0x40` groups; mode 1
  (load save, `FUN_004246c0` from `KONUNG.SA?`) keeps it.
- **Wander radii (`FUN_0042781c`, 0x42781C).** `_DAT_0081D6E0`/`_DAT_0081D6A4`
  are set statically: 800x600 → `20`/`9`; 640x480 → `17`/`7`. `FUN_00413a84`
  samples `rng() % (2*radius)`.

  samples `rng() % (2*radius)`.

## 28.6 Settlement → prop linkage (`FUN_0043adf0`)

For the settlement whose location matches, sub-records (`stride 8`, `+2` = prop
index, low byte = a small id) patch the prop runtime table. The remake parses
settlements for waypoint ownership only; the prop-linkage pass is absent.

## 28.7 WorldObject runtime table (`0x77DEB4`, 500 × 0x40)

Active world objects carry `+0x08`/`+0x0A` runtime-grid coordinates that are run
through the **Entity** projection (`FUN_00438a78`) and drawn in their own depth
pass, with location at the high word of `+0x12`. Only `+0x00` (raw object index)
and `+0x1E` prop field are modeled by the remake (see §25 #7).

## 28.8 Terrain-extent scan (`FUN_0043adf0`)

#### `drawer_konung_general_378655683122ee74f53e2904`

1E` prop field are modeled by the remake (see §25 #7).

## 28.8 Terrain-extent scan (`FUN_0043adf0`)

At load the runtime scans non-zero terrain to compute `_DAT_0081d758`
(`max_row * 32`, vertical extent) and `_DAT_0081d73c` (`(max_col-1) * 116`,
horizontal extent). These bound scrolling/culling. The remake derives extents
from its own lattice constants, not from a data scan.

# 29. `CLIENT.EXE`

`CLIENT.EXE` (939008 bytes, PE32/i386, `machine 0x14C`, `e_lfanew 0x80`) is a
**separate build** from `konung.res` (1586688 bytes). It contains the same
`KONUNG.` / `GAME.` filename strings and the same class of game code, but is
smaller and is an earlier/alternate executable.

# 31. Sound system (`SOUNDS.RES`)

**Status: ORIGINAL-CODE VERIFIED (format/engine).**

- Files: `SOUNDS.RES` (21 981 240 B, 391 clips) and `Sounds_.res` (13 314 264 B,
  244 clips) — same format, partly different content; the game opens
  `SOUNDS.RES` (`FUN_004392B4`).
- Container: 8-byte header (`magic 00 00 00 01` + u32) + `1000 x {u32 offset, u32
  size}` index at offset 8; data base **file offset 8008**; clips are raw signed
  **16-bit little-endian mono PCM** (`frames = size/2`).
- Engine: DirectSound (`DSOUND.dll`). `FUN_00429C0C` creates three secondary
  buffers at **22050 / 19050 / 25050 Hz** (mono 16-bit) = pitch variants;
  `FUN_0042A13C(id, x, y, z)` copies a preloaded clip (`0x81A990[id]`) and plays

#### `drawer_konung_general_be5e959eab56f97c4b853688`

- **room:** general

- **filed_at:** 2026-10-21T19:40:17.273972
- **added_by:** mempalace
- **hall:** creative
- **chunk_index:** 87
- **chunk_total:** 93

) = pitch variants;
  `FUN_0042A13C(id, x, y, z)` copies a preloaded clip (`0x81A990[id]`) and plays
  it; `FUN_0042987C` shuts down. NPC voice ids are `0x0C..0x13`.
- Remake: `web/src/sound.rs` (decoder); ABI `KIND_SOUNDS = 14`; manifest field
  `sounds`; a `PlayableApp.sounds` queue drained by `www/app.js` into WebAudio.
  Report `SOUND_SYSTEM.md`.

# Konung quest/dialogue script opcodes — reference

Complete catalogue of the opcodes used by the `QUEST_R.RES` dialogue
**condition** (`B4`) and **action** (`B5`) programs, recovered from `konung.res`
(Ghidra 12.1.4; sha256 `d698c4f2…679810`). The machine-readable source of truth
is [`web/src/script.rs`](web/src/script.rs); this document mirrors it and adds the
low-opcode families, the usage census and the execution model.

## 1. Program encoding and dispatch

A program is a `u16` list **terminated by `0xFFFF`**. Each word carries an operand
in its low 9 bits and flags in the high bits:

#### `drawer_konung_general_20b1bcca28dd25e154aabcd8`

- **wing:** konung
- **room:** general
- **source_file:** C:\Users\z10ba\AppData\Local\Temp\opencode\konung-docs\subsystems\QUEST_OPCODES.md
- **filed_at:** 2026-10-21T19:40:19.802012
- **added_by:** mempalace
- **hall:** technical
- **chunk_index:** 1
- **chunk_total:** 24

terminated by `0xFFFF`**. Each word carries an operand
in its low 9 bits and flags in the high bits:

| bits | meaning |
|---|---|
| `0x01FF` | operand |
| `0x2000` | operand is a **script opcode** → `FUN_0043159C(operand)` |
| `0x4000` | condition group separator (only the condition evaluator `FUN_00434FE8`) |
| `0x8000` | negate the term / set the `B7` flag slot `operand` |

A word with `0x2000` clear and no `0x2000`/`0x4000` is a `B7` flag test/set. The
**same opcodes are shared** by conditions (`FUN_00434FE8`) and actions
(`FUN_00434BB8`); a handler that only makes sense as a test is a *predicate*, one
with a side effect is an *action*.

`FUN_0043159C(op)`:

#### `drawer_konung_general_3d63e287bb0a052639bd62f0`

- **wing:** konung
- **room:** general
- **source_file:** C:\Users\z10ba\AppData\Local\Temp\opencode\konung-docs\subsystems\QUEST_OPCODES.md
- **filed_at:** 2026-10-21T19:40:19.804014
- **added_by:** mempalace
- **hall:** consciousness
- **chunk_index:** 2
- **chunk_total:** 24

makes sense as a test is a *predicate*, one
with a side effect is an *action*.

`FUN_0043159C(op)`:

```text
op < 0x38  -> FUN_00432F8C(op + 1)      # group predicate      (N = op+1)
op < 0x70  -> FUN_00433FF8(op - 0x37)   # group member predicate (N = op-0x37)
op < 0xA6  -> FUN_0043395C(op - 0x6F)   # remove party member  (N = op-0x6F)
op < 0xDC  -> FUN_004347D0(op - 0xA5)   # spawn entity         (N = op-0xA5)
op < 0x112 -> FUN_00433A8C(op - 0xDB)   # party-class predicate (N = op-0xDB)
else          CALL [op*4 + 0x465BD4]    # op-indexed handler table
```

The `0x465BD4` table is **indexed by `op`**, so its entries below `0x112` are
unused filler; the real table begins at `0x46601C`. Handlers exist for
`0x112..0x16E` (higher entries are zero/unused).

real table begins at `0x46601C`. Handlers exist for
`0x112..0x16E` (higher entries are zero/unused).

## 2. Low-opcode families (`0x00..0x111`)

These are *parameterized families*: the opcode selects the function family and
the operand `N` selects the group/class/subtype.

| op range | handler | N | kind | meaning |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `0x00..0x37` | `FUN_00432F8C` | `op+1` | predicate | group/location `N` has an active member (group table `0x67C08C`, stride `0x10`; `+0x0C` high byte == N, `+0x0F & 0x40`, then `+0x0E < +0x0C` or a member with `archetype & 0x80`) |
| `0x38..0x6F` | `FUN_00433FF8` | `op-0x37` | predicate | group `N` member-state predicate (finds no non-hidden/live member) |

#### `drawer_konung_general_366193be8c377fc646521522`

- **filed_at:** 2026-10-21T19:40:19.807049
- **added_by:** mempalace
- **hall:** technical
- **chunk_index:** 4
- **chunk_total:** 24

3FF8` | `op-0x37` | predicate | group `N` member-state predicate (finds no non-hidden/live member) |
| `0x70..0xA5` | `FUN_0043395C` | `op-0x6F` | action | remove the party member whose entity **class** `N` and kind `0x0B` from the party list (`_DAT_0081D56C + 0x70`), then compact |
| `0xA6..0xDB` | `FUN_004347D0` | `op-0xA5` | action | spawn a new entity **kind `0x0B`, class/subtype `N`** into the world |
| `0xDC..0x111` | `FUN_00433A8C` | `op-0xDB` | predicate | the party contains a member of entity **class** `N` (kind `0x0B`); returns `slot+1` |

`N` domains: group ops `1..0x38`; party/spawn ops `1..0x36`.

## 4. `B7` flag slots

Block `B7` of `QUEST_R.RES` is **300 four-byte slots** at `0x615068`:

```text
+0x00 u8   flags      (bit 0x80 = "active/set")
+0x01 u8   unknown
+0x02 u16  journal text line (index into B6)
```

`FUN_00434BB8` sets/clears the `0x80` bit, `FUN_00434FE8` reads it (with the
`0x8000` negation flag). The quest-log renderer `FUN_0042CBD0` case 2 prints a
slot when `flags & 0x80` and its `+0x02` line != `0xFFFF`. Slots are initialised
from the file and mutated at runtime.

# 5. Trade / loot

**`0x113`** (`FUN_00431E2C`) is the only opcode that opens an inventory exchange:

1. `_DAT_0081D694 = [player + 0x34]` (money); scans the player inventory
   (`+0x70`) and adds 10 to `_DAT_0081D694` per item of class `0xB`, subtype `0x20`.
2. Copies the player inventory (`[player + 0x72]`, `0x40` bytes) into UI grid
   `0x81CB24`.
3. Resolves the partner role with `FUN_0041182C(_DAT_0081D574)`:
   - no owned waypoint → `_DAT_0081D63C = 6`, partner money `_DAT_0081D698 =
     [partner + 0x34]`, partner inventory (`[partner + 0x72]`) → `0x81CBA4`;
   - otherwise the settlement stock list (`+0x412`/`+0x444`/`+0x484`), filtered
     by `+0x4B2 & 4` and item class > 2; partner money = `99999`.
4. `FUN_00430D6C(0)` opens exchange **interface mode 0**.

d item class > 2; partner money = `99999`.
4. `FUN_00430D6C(0)` opens exchange **interface mode 0**.

Related but distinct: `0x124` only *grants* a quest item; `0x123`/`0x14C`/`0x162`/
`0x16B` manage world/party actors; `0x158`/`0x16C` only set the interface mode
byte `+0xD2`.

4`. Implemented ops:

  predicates `0x115`, `0x12E`, `0x12F`, `0x131..0x136`, `0x138`, `0x15D`,

- `FUN_00434FE8` semantics (verified): `bVar2` starts `true`; a term with `0x4000`
  is ANDed only while the result is still true (short-circuit AND); a term without
  `0x4000` restarts the result only once it has failed (OR-alternative). The
  shipped file sets `0x4000` on **every** condition word, so conditions are a
  plain AND. `0x8000` negates a term; `0x2000` marks an opcode predicate.
- Trade/loot (`0x113`) and other unlisted opcodes remain no-ops.

# Konung sound system — reverse-engineered notes

Target: `konung.res` (code) + `SOUNDS.RES` / `Sounds_.res` (data).

## 1. Files

Two variants of the same archive share one container format:

| File | Size | Occupied clips |
|---|---|---|
| `SOUNDS.RES` | 21 981 240 | 391 |
| `Sounds_.res` | 13 314 264 | 244 |

They are **not identical**: 197 ids are common (145 byte-identical, some differ in
size) and each has ids the other lacks. The game opens `SOUNDS.RES`
(`FUN_004392B4`, string `0x450690`).

## 2. Container format

#### `drawer_konung_general_2a3b1baa62e78327af48ccf4`

- **wing:** konung
- **room:** general
- **source_file:** C:\Users\z10ba\AppData\Local\Temp\opencode\konung-docs\subsystems\SOUND_SYSTEM.md
- **filed_at:** 2026-10-21T19:40:20.520901
- **added_by:** mempalace
- **hall:** creative
- **chunk_index:** 1
- **chunk_total:** 6

ther lacks. The game opens `SOUNDS.RES`
(`FUN_004392B4`, string `0x450690`).

## 2. Container format

```text
u8  magic[4]   = 00 00 00 01
u32 header_word                    // unresolved
Entry index[1000]                  // at file offset 8, 8 bytes each
    u32 data_offset                // relative to DATA_BASE
    u32 data_size
data_region[...]                   // raw signed 16-bit little-endian MONO PCM
```

The index occupies `8..8008`; the **data region starts at file offset 8008**
(`DATA_BASE`). `data_offset == 0xFFFFFFFF` marks an unused slot. Each clip is raw
`s16` mono PCM; its byte length is `frames * 2`.

Verified against `SOUNDS.RES`: id 0 = offset 0, size 66298; ids are contiguous;
the NPC voice ids `0x0C..0x13` are all present and frame-aligned.

## 3. Engine + playback

- **DirectSound** (`DSOUND.dll`, `DirectSoundCreate`). The engine object lives at
  `_DAT_0082D244` (vtable); `FUN_00429C0C` initialises it and creates **three**
  secondary buffers with `WAVEFORMATEX` records at `0x82D28C` / `0x82D29E` /
  `0x82D2B0`:
  - `nSamplesPerSec` **22050**, `nAvgBytesPerSec` 44100, mono, 16-bit
  - `nSamplesPerSec` **19050**, `nAvgBytesPerSec` 38092, mono, 16-bit
  - `nSamplesPerSec` **25050**, `nAvgBytesPerSec` 50100, mono, 16-bit
  The three rates are effectively **pitch variants**; playback selects one
  (`FUN_0042A13C` chooses buffer 0 for low non-UI ids, else `rng()%3`).
- **Playback** `FUN_0042A13C(id, x, y, z)`: locks the chosen buffer, copies the

#### `drawer_konung_general_c7b52cb4ea0096e4ada70cdb`

- **wing:** konung
- **room:** general
- **source_file:** C:\Users\z10ba\AppData\Local\Temp\opencode\konung-docs\subsystems\SOUND_SYSTEM.md
- **filed_at:** 2026-10-21T19:40:20.522906
- **added_by:** mempalace
- **hall:** creative
- **chunk_index:** 3
- **chunk_total:** 6

ds, else `rng()%3`).
- **Playback** `FUN_0042A13C(id, x, y, z)`: locks the chosen buffer, copies the
  clip's PCM from the preloaded block `0x81A990[id]` (size `0x6213DC[id]`), sets
  volume/pan (`param_3`/`param_2`) and starts it. Up to 45 concurrent buffers.
- **Shutdown** `FUN_0042987C`.
- The interface loader `FUN_004392B4` preloads ids **0..55** into `0x81A990`.
  NPC greeting voice ids are `0x0C..0x13` (selected in `FUN_00435290` by the NPC
  archetype: humanoids `rng()%4 + 0x0C`).

## 4. Remake integration

- `web/src/sound.rs` decodes the archive (`SoundArchive::parse`, `entry`,
  `clip_bytes`, `frame_count`, `duration_ms`); constants `DATA_BASE = 8008`,
  `SAMPLE_RATES = [22050, 19050, 25050]`, `BITS = 16`, `CHANNELS = 1`.
- Loaded as `KIND_SOUNDS = 14`; manifest field `sounds`.
- The runtime queues requested clip ids in `PlayableApp.sounds` (drained each
  frame, like the text queue). A dialogue open pushes the NPC voice id.
- ABI: `sound_count`/`sound_id` (queue) and `debug_sound_clip_ptr/len/rate`
  (PCM access). The browser shell (`www/app.js`) builds a mono `AudioBuffer` on
  first use (base rate 22050) and plays it via WebAudio; the context is created
  on the first user gesture.

# Konung keyboard / key-press input — reverse-engineered notes

Target: `konung.res` (shipped PE32/i386, image base `0x400000`,
`sha256 d698c4f231489a372cc25212ab2819720a36d7ce5307a045db684135cf679810`).
All addresses are **virtual addresses in `konung.res`** unless stated otherwise.
This document is **authoritative to the original binary**; the remake is not
consulted for semantics here (see §10 for the only cross-reference).

Companion document: `MOUSE_INPUT_SYSTEM.md` describes the mouse half of the same
pipeline. This file describes only the **keyboard** half: every Win32 key
message, the ring buffer, the per-frame consumer, the exact VK dispatch, and
every gate that can swallow a keystroke.

## 0. Pipeline overview

```
   Windows USER32 message queue   (class "Konung", registered in FUN_0042B1A8)
                 │
                 ▼
(A)  WndProc  ~0x42B65C  (range tree @ 0x42B92A; NOT a Ghidra function)
       WM_KEYDOWN 0x0100 @ 0x42B6F6  ->  enqueue(wParam, flag=1)
       WM_KEYUP   0x0101 @ 0x42B737  ->  enqueue(0x10|0x11, flag=0)   [only Shift/Ctrl]
                 │
                 ▼
     input event ring:  ev[16] u32 @ 0x81C154, flags[16] u8 @ 0x81CF70
                        write idx _DAT_0081D66C, read idx _DAT_0081D668 (& 0xF)
                 │
     frame tick  WM_USER 0x400  (60 ms budget, _DAT_0081D5C8 = 0x3C)
                 ▼
(B)  FUN_00436214   pops exactly ONE event per tick;

udget, _DAT_0081D5C8 = 0x3C)
                 ▼
(B)  FUN_00436214   pops exactly ONE event per tick;
        - updates Shift/Ctrl flags _81D624 / _81D5E4
        - consumes ESC (0x1B) and runs the _81D5AC screen switch
        - in gameplay (screen 0, not game-over) and gated by _81D61C bit 0:
              if (leftoverVK != 0) FUN_00435B0C(leftoverVK);
              FUN_0042260C();                       (mouse/UI)
                 ▼
(C)  FUN_00435B0C(vk)   the keyboard command dispatcher (sole caller 0x436214)
```

#### `drawer_konung_general_5d88f0303a87ea45a467c6cb`

- **wing:** konung

▼
(C)  FUN_00435B0C(vk)   the keyboard command dispatcher (sole caller 0x436214)
```

**Timing / capacity consequence.** Because the producer enqueues on every
`WM_KEYDOWN` but the consumer pops **one event per 60 ms frame** (~16.7 Hz),
at most one keystroke is acted on per frame; the 16-slot ring absorbs a short
burst. There is **no queue-full check** — the producer only ever does
`w = (w+1) & 0xF`, and "empty" is encoded as `read == write`, so a full ring and
an empty ring are indistinguishable.

---

## 1. Stage A — Win32 key messages

WndProc is a 4-argument stdcall; `wParam = [ebp+0x1C]`, `msg = [ebp+0x18]`.
The range tree at `0x42B92A` routes the key messages (see
`MOUSE_INPUT_SYSTEM.md` §1.2 for the full table):

ge tree at `0x42B92A` routes the key messages (see
`MOUSE_INPUT_SYSTEM.md` §1.2 for the full table):

| message | value | handler @ | effect (ORIGINAL-CODE VERIFIED) |
|---|---|---|---|
| WM_KEYDOWN | `0x0100` | `0x42B6F6` | `EAX = [0x81D66C]`; `flags[EAX] @0x81CF70 = 1`; `EAX = [0x81D66C]`; `ev[EAX*4] @0x81C154 = wParam`; `w = (w+1) & 0xF`; if `_81D5F4 != 0` → `FUN_0042B098()` |
| WM_KEYUP | `0x0101` | `0x42B737` | `wParam = [ebp+0x1C]`; **only** `0x10` (VK_SHIFT) or `0x11` (VK_CONTROL) are enqueued, always with `flags = 0` |

Decoded instructions:

Decoded instructions:

```
0x42B6F6  MOV EAX,[0x0081D66C]                    ; ring write index
0x42B6FB  MOV byte ptr [EAX+0x0081CF70],0x1        ; flags[w] = 1
0x42B702  MOV EAX,[0x0081D66C]                    ; reload index
          ... store wParam into ev[w], w=(w+1)&0xF, call FUN_0042B098 ...
0x42B737  MOV EAX,[EBP+0x1C]                      ; WM_KEYUP: read wParam
```

Consequences:

#### `drawer_konung_general_7523290892e4afd2571ec5ce`

- **wing:** konung
- **room:** general
- **source_file:** C:\Users\z10ba\AppData\Local\Temp\opencode\konung-docs-v3\input\KEY_INPUT_SYSTEM.md
- **filed_at:** 2026-10-21T20:12:29.259486

098 ...
0x42B737  MOV EAX,[EBP+0x1C]                      ; WM_KEYUP: read wParam
```

Consequences:

- **Any** key-down is recorded; the dispatcher, not WndProc, decides meaning.
- Key-up is **ignored** except for `VK_SHIFT`/`VK_CONTROL`, which are the only
  two "level" modifiers the game tracks (no Alt, no per-key release, no
  auto-repeat filtering — auto-repeat arrives as repeated WM_KEYDOWNs).
- No DirectInput; ordinary window messages only.

### 1.1 Ring buffer

| symbol | address | type | role |
|---|---|---|---|
| `ev[16]` | `0x81C154` | u32 × 16 | raw `wParam` (VK) per slot |
| `flags[16]` | `0x81CF70` | u8 × 16 | 1 = key-down, 0 = key-up |
| `_DAT_0081D66C` | `0x81D66C` | u32 | write index |
| `_DAT_0081D668` | `0x81D668` | u32 | read index |

| `_DAT_0081D668` | `0x81D668` | u32 | read index |

Effective size **16** (`& 0xF`). Producer and consumer both wrap identically.

## 2. Stage B — per-frame consumer `FUN_00436214`

Runs once per 60 ms tick (`WM_USER 0x400`, `ev_82f5d720…`). The key-related
portion is at the top of the function:

```c
r = _DAT_0081D668;
if (r == _DAT_0081D66C) {          /* empty */
    code = 0;
} else {
    flag = *(u8*)(r + 0x81CF70);
    _DAT_0081D668 = (r + 1) & 0xF;
    code = *(u32*)(r*4 + 0x81C154);
    if (flag == 0) {                       /* WM_KEYUP */
        if (code == 0x10)      _DAT_0081D624 = 0;              /* Shift up  */
        else if (code == 0x11) _DAT_0081D5E4 = 0;              /* Ctrl  up  */

#### `drawer_konung_general_ad7a4c026f59bc364ee14751`

- **wing:** konung
- **room:** general
- **source_file:** C:\Users\z10ba\AppData\Local\Temp\opencode\konung-docs-v3\input\KEY_INPUT_SYSTEM.md
- **filed_at:** 2026-10-21T20:12:29.261500
- **added_by:** opencode
- **hall:** technical
- **chunk_index:** 10
- **chunk_total:** 52

/* Shift up  */
        else if (code == 0x11) _DAT_0081D5E4 = 0;              /* Ctrl  up  */
    } else {                               /* WM_KEYDOWN */
        if (code == 0x10) { _DAT_0081D624 = 1; code = 0; }     /* Shift down, consumed */
        else if (code == 0x11) { _DAT_0081D5E4 = 1; code = 0; }/* Ctrl  down, consumed */
        else if (code == 0x1B) {                               /* ESC, consumed */
            code = 0;
            switch (_DAT_0081D5AC) { ... }                     /* see §2.1 */
        }
    }
}
```

So `code` (the "leftover" VK) is **0** for Shift/Ctrl/ESC and is the raw VK for
every other key. `_DAT_0081D624` = Shift level, `_DAT_0081D5E4` = Ctrl level.

and is the raw VK for
every other key. `_DAT_0081D624` = Shift level, `_DAT_0081D5E4` = Ctrl level.

Then the function latches the mouse button edges, integrates the cursor delta
(`FUN_00428C00`), and runs `switch(_DAT_0081D5AC)` on the screen state. The
**gameplay** case (screen `0`, game not over) is where keys are dispatched:

```c
case 0:
  ... world/interface update (FUN_00420F30, FUN_00421B50, FUN_00424520, FUN_0041D048) ...
  if (!game_over) {
      if ((DAT_0081D61C & 1) == 0) {
          if (leftoverVK != 0) FUN_00435B0C(leftoverVK);      /* call site 0x4367E5 */
          FUN_0042260C();                                     /* mouse/UI */
      } else {
          DAT_0081D61C ^= 1;
          if (leftoverVK != 0) FUN_00435B0C(leftoverVK);      /* call site 0x43680C */

`FUN_00435B0C` has **exactly one caller** (`0x436214`) and two call sites
(`0x4367E5`, `0x43680C`) — both inside this gameplay branch.

### 2.1 ESC screen switch (`switch (_DAT_0081D5AC)`)

ESC is handled in Stage B, **before** the screen switch, so it works on any
screen (not only gameplay) and is consumed (never reaches `FUN_00435B0C`).
`_81D5F8` = "game was running", `_81D6B8` = which menu page, `_81D610` =
interface mode.

00435B0C`).
`_81D5F8` = "game was running", `_81D6B8` = which menu page, `_81D610` =
interface mode.

| `_81D5AC` | action |
|---|---|
| `0` (in-game) | `FUN_00428E98()`; if `_81D610 == 1` (dialogue) nothing; else if `_81D610 == 0` → `_81D5AC = 7; _81D5F8 = 1; _81D6B8 = 0` (**open main menu**, remember game running); else → `_81D610 = 0; _81D680 = 0; FUN_00428F94()` (**close the interface window**) |
| `1` | if `_81D5F8 != 0` → `_81D5AC = 0; _81D6B0 = 0; FUN_0042C384(); FUN_0042CBD0(-1); FUN_00428F94()` (**resume the running game**) |
| `2` | `_81D5AC = 7; _81D5F8 = 0; _81D6B8 = 2` (menu page 2) |
| `4`, `5` | `_81D5AC = 7; _81D6B8 = 5` (menu page 5) |
| `6` | `_81D5AC = 7; _81D6B8 = 6`; copies state `_81D5AC..` → `_81D5D4/_5EC/_604/_60C/_620/_62C` from `0x831C54..` (menu page 6) |

The ESC switch is separate from the *outer* `switch(_81D5AC)` screen dispatch in
the same function (which handles the per-screen game loop, including cases `8`
map-load and `9` poster); those outer cases are reached by the normal frame
flow, not by ESC.

`_DAT_0081D6B8` is the menu page selector; `_DAT_0081D5AC` is the top-level
screen id. (Cross-referenced with `MOUSE_INPUT_SYSTEM.md` §0/§6.)

## 3. Stage C — the key dispatcher `FUN_00435B0C(vk)`

Body `0x435B0C`–`0x436211` (non-contiguous; ~1778 bytes). Sole caller
`FUN_00436214`. Input `vk` is the raw Win32 virtual-key code that survived the
`code != 0` filter. Two global gates are read throughout:

- `_DAT_0081D624` — **Shift** level (set by Shift key-down in Stage B).
- `_DAT_0081D5E4` — **Ctrl** level (set by Ctrl key-down in Stage B).
- `_DAT_0081D638` — **debug/cheat enable** flag (see §5).
- Selection list `_DAT_0081A814[0..9]` (party pointers; `[0] = _DAT_0081D56C`,
  the controlled hero / leader).

The dispatch is a nested range tree on `vk`. The complete, literal behaviour:

#### `drawer_konung_general_f30dd9c27873fc1ba0b57384`

- **wing:** konung
- **room:** general
- **source_file:** C:\Users\z10ba\AppData\Local\Temp\opencode\konung-docs-v3\input\KEY_INPUT_SYSTEM.md
- **filed_at:** 2026-10-21T20:12:29.268042
- **added_by:** opencode
- **hall:** general
- **chunk_index:** 16
- **chunk_total:** 52

olled hero / leader).

The dispatch is a nested range tree on `vk`. The complete, literal behaviour:

```c
void FUN_00435B0C(uint vk) {
  if (vk < 0x49) {
    if (vk < 0x33) {
      if (vk < 0x30) {
        if (vk < 9) return;                       /* 0x00..0x08 ignored */
        if (vk > 9) {
          if (vk != 0x20) return;                 /* 0x0A..0x1F ignored (except SPACE) */
          FUN_00421554(); return;                 /* SPACE — battle/peace stance */
        }
        /* vk == 0x09 TAB */  ...see §4 TAB...    return;
      }
      /* 0x30..0x32  '0' '1' '2' */
      if (vk > 0x30) {                            /* '1','2' */
        if (vk > 0x31) { if (_81D638) _81D764 = 2; FUN_004216DC(0); return; }  /* '2' */

#### `drawer_konung_general_aa29ee8abbe5f7c1237867ff`

- **wing:** konung

> Note on the **two** debug tails: `_81D764 = vk - 0x30` is the tail of the
> `vk < 0x49` branch (reached by `'0'`..`'9'`, giving 0..9);
> `_81D764 = vk - 0x37` is `LAB_00435C8D` (reached by the `goto` for
> `'B'`..`'H'` except `'F'`, and by fall-through for `'J'`, giving
> `0xB..0x11` and `0x13`). Only the written values are observed; the field's
> meaning is **UNKNOWN** (§5).

Literal key table

Addresses are the handler functions; "gate" lists what must hold for the action.

| VK (hex) | key | handler | action (observed) | gate |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `0x09` | TAB | inline | cycle the active party selection: if 0–1 selected, select leader / next; if ≥2, clear (`FUN_0043EAD0(0x81A814,0,0x28)`); `FUN_0042CBD0(-1)`; if mode 3 or `_81D600` set → `FUN_0042E520` (open detail) | gameplay |
| `0x1B` | ESC | Stage B | screen/menu switch (§2.1) | any screen |
| `0x20` | SPACE | `FUN_00421554` | toggle battle/peace stance of selected units (`+0x00 ∓6`, `+0x03 ^= 4`, re-idle `FUN_00414078`/`FUN_0041435C`) | gameplay |
| `0x31` | `1` | `FUN_004216DC(2)` | select weapon slot `+0x6A` (`Entity+0x12 = 2`) | gameplay |

elected unit (`FUN_0042E520`) | mode 0 only |

Ignored ranges/keys: `0x00`–`0x08`, `0x0A`–`0x1F` (except SPACE), `0x3A`–`0x40`,
`0x4B` `K`, `0x4E` `N`, `0x50` `P`, `0x52` `R`, `0x55`–`0x57`, `0x59` `Y`,
`0x6C` (separator), and all VKs above `0xC0`.

> The literal mapping `1→slot 2`, `2→slot 0`, `3→slot 1` is **exactly what the
> binary does** (`FUN_004216DC` picks `+0x66` for arg 0, `+0x68` for arg 1,
> `+0x6A` for arg 2, and cycles for arg ≥ 3). The documented `scroll-slot`
> semantics of the three weapon fields are external; this doc only asserts the
> observed arithmetic.

### 3.2 TAB tail (selection / detail)

```c
_DAT_0081D708 = 0; _DAT_0081D670 = 0;
n = count selected (up to 10, stop at first null);
if (n < 2) {
    if (n == 0) _DAT_0081A814 = _DAT_0081D56C;              /* select leader */
    else { advance to the next occupant record; if past the end, wrap to leader }
} else {
    FUN_0043EAD0(&DAT_0081A814, 0, 0x28);                    /* clear selection (10 ptrs) */
}
FUN_0042CBD0(-1);                                            /* redraw world */
if (_DAT_0081D610 == 3) { _DAT_0081D560 = _DAT_0081A814; ... FUN_0042E520 ... return; }
if (_DAT_0081D600 == 0) return;
_DAT_0081D560 = _DAT_0081A814; FUN_0042E520(_DAT_0081A814, 0);
```

### 3.3 `0xC0` (`~`) tail

Identical "open/close detail" logic but gated on `_81D610 == 0`, and toggles
`_81D600`/`_81D630` when the selected unit is already the current detail target.

---

## 4. Handler cross-reference (what each key ultimately does)

| handler | verified behaviour |
|---|---|
| `FUN_00421554` | SPACE. The direction comes from the **first selected** unit's `+0x03` battle bit. For every selected, non-empty, living unit (`+0x04 & 0x80 == 0`, action not `3`/`9`): if entering battle and the unit's bit is clear → `+0x00 -= 6`, `+0x03 |= 4`; if leaving and the unit's bit is set → `+0x00 = min(+6,6)`, clear approach bits `+0x02 &= 0xFA` (set `0x40` if zero), `+0x03 ^= 4`; then re-idle via `FUN_00414078`/

r approach bits `+0x02 &= 0xFA` (set `0x40` if zero), `+0x03 ^= 4`; then re-idle via `FUN_00414078`/`FUN_0041435C`. Plays sound 1/0xF or 1/0xA by resulting stance. Unselected units are untouched. |
| `FUN_004216DC(arg)` | Select/cycle the active weapon slot `Entity+0x12`: arg 0 → `+0x66`, arg 1 → `+0x68`, arg 2 → `+0x6A`, arg ≥3 → advance to the next non-empty of the three, with battle-stance bookkeeping. Sounds `FUN_0042A13C(4)`, `FUN_00428DB0(0,…)`. |
| `FUN_00421458` | `A`. Sets `_DAT_0081CF69 = 10`; for every non-empty unit: zero `+0x06/+0x07`, set `+0x02 = 'D'` if `+0x0F>>24 == 1` else `'A'`, `+0x14 = 0xFF`, and if not in battle-stance enter it (`+0x00 -= 6`, `+0x03 |= 4`, `FUN_00414078`). Sound 4/7. |

## 5. Global state reference

| symbol | address | role |
|---|---|---|
| `_DAT_0081D5AC` | `0x81D5AC` | top-level screen id (0 = in-game; 1..9 menus/screens) |
| `_DAT_0081D610` | `0x81D610` | interface mode (0 world / 1 dialogue / 2 quest-log / 3 character / **5 world map-travel** / 7 detail) |
| `_DAT_0081D624` | `0x81D624` | **Shift** level flag (from VK 0x10 down/up) |
| `_DAT_0081D5E4` | `0x81D5E4` | **Ctrl** level flag (from VK 0x11 down/up) |
| `_DAT_0081D638` | `0x81D638` | **debug/cheat enable** (default 0; reset on load; toggled Shift+Ctrl+L) |
| `_DAT_0081D764` | `0x81D764` | debug numeric selector target (written only when `_81D638`; also `= 1` at init `0x42B37F`). Meaning **UNKNOWN** |

### 5.1 `_DAT_0081D638` — debug/cheat flag (evidence)

- Reset to `0` on game/asset load: `FUN_004392B4` @`0x4392CA`
  (`ev_662fb66a…`), which also resets `_81D634`.
- Toggled by **Shift+Ctrl+L** in `FUN_00435B0C` (key case `0x4C`).
- When set, `FUN_004182D4` (the "can this unit meet this item/level
  requirement" predicate) short-circuits to `1`/true for every case
  (`ev_21ccd9d4…`), i.e. **requirements are bypassed**.
- When set, the digit/letter keys write `_81D764` (see §3). `_81D764` is
  initialized to `1` at startup `0x42B37F` (`ev_ecb02cbd…`) and has no other
  writers besides the debug selector. Its consumer/meaning is **UNKNOWN**;
  `xrefs(0x81D638)` returns `0x4182E0`, `0x41C3D8`, `0x435B23..0x435D72`,

## 6. Gates summary (why a key can be swallowed)

1. **Ring** — every key-down is stored, but only **one** is consumed per 60 ms
   frame; a full ring aliases "empty", and there is no overflow guard.
2. **Key-up** — ignored unless Shift/Ctrl.
3. **`code != 0`** — Shift/Ctrl/ESC are cleared to 0 in Stage B and never reach
   `FUN_00435B0C`.
4. **Screen** — `FUN_00435B0C` is called **only** in gameplay (`_81D5AC == 0`
   and not game-over); in menus/screens the only key handled is ESC. Non-
   gameplay screens are mouse-driven (`FUN_0042260C`).

#### `drawer_konung_general_9ac0e755303c17ff77af6afd`

- **wing:** konung
- **room:** general
- **source_file:** C:\Users\z10ba\AppData\Local\Temp\opencode\konung-docs-v3\input\KEY_INPUT_SYSTEM.md
- **filed_at:** 2026-10-21T20:12:29.292257
- **added_by:** opencode
- **hall:** creative
- **chunk_index:** 39
- **chunk_total:** 52

nus/screens the only key handled is ESC. Non-
   gameplay screens are mouse-driven (`FUN_0042260C`).
5. **Modal gate** — the whole gameplay input block is gated by `_81D61C` bit 0
   (which is also toggled in place around the two `FUN_00435B0C` call sites).
6. **Modifiers** — Shift (`_81D624`) / Ctrl (`_81D5E4`) are level flags; several
   keys are modifier-gated (Shift+A select-all, Ctrl+S, Shift+Ctrl+L) and some
   are modifier-suppressed (a Shift-gated branch may shadow the plain action).
7. **Interface mode** — `I`/`M`/`Q`/`T` each require specific `_81D610` states.
8. **Debug flag** — all `_81D764` writes require `_81D638`.

## 8. CHECK-list answers (KONUNG_SOURCE_OF_TRUTH.md §0.1)

- **CHECK 1 — Which coordinate/data domain?** `wParam` is a raw Win32
  **virtual-key code** (`0x00–0xFF`), not a scancode and not an ASCII char.
  `FUN_00435B0C` compares it directly against VK constants. The only domain
  conversions are the debug-index arithmetic `vk - 0x30` / `vk - 0x37`.
- **CHECK 2 — What exact original routine consumes it?**
  `FUN_00436214` (Stage B) pops the ring and calls **`FUN_00435B0C(vk)`**; ESC
  is consumed by Stage B itself. Ring producer is the WndProc at `0x42B6F6`
  (key-down) and `0x42B737` (key-up, Shift/Ctrl only).
- **CHECK 3 — Is there an explicit conversion before use?** No scaling,

0` states.

## 7. Timing invariants

- Frame budget **60 ms** (`_81D5C8 = 0x3C`, set at `0x42B32F`; limiter
  `0x42B61A`; `timeGetTime`). Stage B runs once per budget.
- One ring event consumed per frame ⇒ ≤ 1 key command per 60 ms.
- `_DAT_0081D634` and `_DAT_0081D764` persist across frames until rewritten.

## 10. Remake parity (non-authoritative)

The remake does **not** use the Win32 VK dispatch. Input enters through the
browser ABI: `web/src/abi/input.rs::key_down(code)` routes a DOM
`keydown.code` to either the frontend (`frontend/mod.rs::key_down`) or the live
game (`app/input.rs::PlayableApp::key_down`), which is a **flat `match` on a
small set of ad-hoc constants** (`web/src/constants/keys.rs`). There is **no
input ring, no one-per-frame throttle, no Shift/Ctrl level tracking inside the
router** (Shift arrives as a separate `set_shift` call from
`Event.shiftKey`), and no `_81D61C`/debug gating. Keys act immediately on each
DOM event.

`web/src/app/input.rs::key_down` map (verbatim):

Structural differences to remember when checking “parity”:

- Original Shift **is** the additive-selection modifier (`_DAT_0081D624`,
  consumed in `FUN_00424D8C` and Shift+A); the remake reproduces this for
  **mouse clicking** only (`abi::set_shift` → `select_party_member`).
- Original key commands are **gameplay-only** and throttled to one per 60 ms;
  the remake runs them on every DOM event regardless of the frontend/game
  split, but each handler is itself gated (e.g. `if !self.mode_graph`, or
  consumed while `dialog.is_some()`).
- The remake deliberately diverges for browser ergonomics (`C`/`V`/`H`), which
  is why the AGENTS.md note calls `TAB` a remake binding.

## 11. Residual unknowns

1. **`_DAT_0081D764`** — written by the debug selector and initialized to 1;
   its consumer/meaning is unresolved (`xrefs(0x81D764)` shows only writers
   plus no obvious reader). **UNKNOWN.**
2. **`_DAT_0081D634`** — incremented/decremented by numpad `*`/`+`/`-`
   (0..2). Whether this is a camera zoom, scroll step, or party size is
   unresolved. **UNKNOWN.**
3. **`_DAT_0081D564`** — Ctrl+S toggles it between `0` and `_DAT_0081D56C`
   (leader pointer). Its role is unresolved. **UNKNOWN.**
4. **`_DAT_0081D638`** — characterized as a debug/cheat flag (bypasses equip
   requirements, enables the numeric selector); read at `0x4182E0`/`0x41C3D8`

# Konung mouse / input dispatch — reverse-engineered notes

Target: `konung.res` (shipped PE32/i386, image base `0x400000`,
`sha256 d698c4f231489a372cc25212ab2819720a36d7ce5307a045db684135cf679810`).
All addresses are virtual addresses in `konung.res` unless stated otherwise.

This document describes the **whole mouse/input pipeline**: how a Win32 mouse
message becomes a per-frame game command, every gate that can intercept it, and
exactly what each click does in the world (empty/passable cell, blocked cell,
NPC, mob, item). It is derived with the REA Ghidra provider (12.1.4) plus a
Capstone disassembly of the un-analysed input region, and cross-checked against
the remake (`web/src/app/input.rs`, `web/src/dialog.rs`).

ed input region, and cross-checked against
the remake (`web/src/app/input.rs`, `web/src/dialog.rs`).

The key top-level state:
- `_DAT_0081D5AC` — application/screen state (0 = in-game; menu/screens otherwise).
  Set to `-1` at startup (`0x42B1B4`), `9` by init (`0x42B2B0`), and driven by the
  ESC switch in `FUN_00436214`.
- `_DAT_0081D610` — **interface mode** (0 world, 1 dialogue, 2 quest-log, 3
  character, 5 world map/travel, 7 inventory-detail). This is the main gate used
  by the command compiler `FUN_004381C8`.

Notes:
- `_DAT_0081D5B4` mirrors `_DAT_0081D5B0` as a "repeat/held" copy (used by the
  edge latch in Stage B).
- The mouse **delta** is `windows_cursor − game_cursor` (see §1.3); it is
  recomputed on every mouse message and on button-down (so a click resyncs after
  a sparse move stream).

### 1.3 Cursor integrator `FUN_00428C00` (called every frame by Stage B)

```
_81D5C0 += _81D5B8          ; cursor_x += dx
_81D5C4 += _81D5BC          ; cursor_y += dy
_81D5BC = 0 ; _81D5B8 = 0   ; consume delta
clamp _81D5C0 to [0, _81D740]
clamp _81D5C4 to [0, _81D75C]
```

So the *visible* command cursor `_81D5C0/_81D5C4` is the Windows pointer,
integrated from deltas and clamped to the viewport rectangle
`[0.._81D740] × [0.._81D75C]` (the detail-window bounds). `FUN_0042A470`
additionally copies `_81D5C0→_81D730` and `_81D5C4→_81D74C` each frame
(`FUN_0042A470` @`0x42AC33`).

### 1.4 Input event ring buffer

- `u32` events at `0x81C154[16]`; byte flags at `0x81CF70[16]`; write index
  `_DAT_0081D66C`, read index `_DAT_0081D668`; **length 16** (index `& 0xF`).
- Enqueue (WM_KEYDOWN, `0x42B6F6`):
  `flags[w] = 1; ev[w] = wParam; w = (w+1) & 0xF;`
- WM_KEYUP enqueues fixed codes `0x10`/`0x11` with `flags[w] = 0`.
- Both wrap and, when `_DAT_0081D5F4 != 0`, call `FUN_0042B098` (renders/limits).

Stage B — per-frame driver `FUN_00436214`

Runs once per 60 ms tick (from the `0x400` message). It:

1. Pops **one** event (`flag = _81CF70[r]; code = _81C154[r]; r = (r+1)&0xF`;
   empty → code 0).
2. Event handling:
   - `flag==0`: `code 0x10 → _DAT_0081D624 = 0`; `code 0x11 → _DAT_0081D5E4 = 0`.
   - `flag!=0`: `code 0x10 → _DAT_0081D624 = 1`; `code 0x11 → _DAT_0081D5E4 = 1`
     (and consume the code); `code 0x1B` (ESC) → screen switch on `_DAT_0081D5AC`.
3. Button-edge latch on `_DAT_0081D5B0` / `_DAT_0081D5B4` → latched edge state
   `_DAT_0081D69C`; it clears press bits of `_DAT_0081D5B0` once consumed.
4. In gameplay (`_DAT_0081D5AC == 0`, game not over) it runs the world/interface
   update (`FUN_00420F30`, `FUN_00421B50`, `FUN_00424520`, `FUN_0041D048`) and

#### `drawer_konung_general_6ba690bb869544681642bbd9`

- **wing:** konung
- **room:** general
- **source_file:** C:\Users\z10ba\AppData\Local\Temp\opencode\konung-docs-v3\input\MOUSE_INPUT_SYSTEM.md
- **filed_at:** 2026-10-21T20:12:30.729004
- **added_by:** opencode
- **hall:** general
- **chunk_index:** 14
- **chunk_total:** 54

s the world/interface
   update (`FUN_00420F30`, `FUN_00421B50`, `FUN_00424520`, `FUN_0041D048`) and
   calls **`FUN_0042260C`** (gated by `_DAT_0081D61C` bit 0 and, just before,
   feeds any leftover key event to `FUN_00435B0C`).
   The keyboard half of this pipeline (ring, VK dispatch, modifiers, gates) is
   documented in **`KEY_INPUT_SYSTEM.md`**.

`_DAT_0081D624` and `_DAT_0081D5E4` are the two keyboard-derived modifier flags
(the only keys WndProc special-cases on key-up are VK `0x10`/`0x11`).

The ESC switch (`_DAT_0081D5AC`) maps the menu/screen states; `_DAT_0081D610`
(the interface mode used by Stage C) is changed by the commands below, not here.

## 3. Stage C — command compiler `FUN_004381C8`

Pure function `int FUN_004381C8(void)` (body `0x4381C8`–`0x43863D`). It reads the
cursor `_DAT_0081D5C0/_DAT_0081D5C4` and returns a **packed command**
`(index << 8) | code`, bailing with `-1` when nothing is under the cursor. Only
two callees:

- `FUN_00438798(x, y, rect_table)` — point-in-rectangle-list test → index or `-1`.
- `FUN_00438900(x, row, off_table, byte_table)` — **weighted in-row column
  lookup**: `off = off_table[row]` (`-1` = absent → 0), then walk `byte_table`
  (`value,weight` pairs, 2-byte stride) accumulating weights until the running
  sum exceeds `x`; returns the `value` byte. Used for the icon/inventory column
  hit-testing.

nning
  sum exceeds `x`; returns the `value` byte. Used for the icon/inventory column
  hit-testing.

Gates by interface mode `_DAT_0081D610`:

| `_81D610` | meaning | command produced |
|---|---|---|
| `5` | **world map / travel** | rectangle test `0x462BEA…`; if hit and discovered-location flag `+0x81CF80[index] != 0` → `index*0x100 + 0x103` (travel), else `-1` |
| `2` | quest log | `FUN_00438798(...,0x45B04C)` → `index*0x100 + 0xCA` |
| `7` | inventory detail | weighted column lookups over `0x465E94`/`0x465E34`; `*0x100 + {0x78,0x79,0x32, 0x1E..0x27}` |
| `default` (0 world / 1 dialog / 3 character) | — | see below |

- If `local` in `0x1E..0x27` (party-member slot) → `(slot-0x1E)*0x100 + 0x1E`.

`FUN_0042260C` (body ≈`0x42260C`–`0x424E0C`, ~34 KB of pseudocode) is entered by
Stage B. Its top level is a **two-branch switch on the mouse buttons**:

Stage B. Its top level is a **two-branch switch on the mouse buttons**:

```
if (_DAT_0081D5B0 & 1) {            /* LEFT button */
    code = FUN_004381C8() & 0xFF;  /* command compiler */
    <left-button command switch>
}
if ((_DAT_0081D5B0 & 2) == 0) {     /* NEITHER button (hover) */
    <hover: cell/object tooltip + hovered-actor name panel + UI tooltips>
}
/* RIGHT button only: */
<right-button world order/interaction switch>
```

`_DAT_0081D578` is the **actor under the cursor** (maintained in the
`0x4236xx`–`0x423Cxx` hit-test region of this function; several of those blocks
were folded away by the decompiler as "unreachable" — see the warnings at the
head of its listing). `_DAT_0081D56C` is the currently controlled hero pointer;
`_DAT_0081A814[0..9]` is the active/party unit list (≤10 pointers).

### 4.1 Left-button command switch (world + interface)

`FUN_004138C8(x,y,type)` (called when `_81D660 != 4`) is a **per-cell marker
spawner**: it finds or creates one 64-byte record in a 200-entry pool at
`0x785BB4` (counter `_DAT_0081D6A8`, cap `0xC8`), keyed by cell x/y, appends the
type to a ≤22-entry list, sets the cell flag bit `0x20` in `DAT_004EE6B0`, and
computes a sprite-centered screen anchor via `FUN_00438A78`.

### 4.6 What each click does (plain-language gates)

| You click… | Left button | Right button |
|---|---|---|
| empty **passable** cell | move every selected unit to the cell (§4.4) | same move, if no actor is targeted (§4.5.1) |
| **blocked / non-passable** cell | destination stored, but the route build fails ⇒ the unit **does not move** (§5) | same |
| **NPC** (human `archetype & 0x40 == 0`, `dialogue_id != 0xFF`) | move only | **open dialogue** (`FUN_00435290` + `FUN_0042CBD0(0)`) |
| **NPC without dialogue id** | move only | party order toward the actor (no dialogue) |
| **mob / enemy** (`archetype & 0x40`) | move only | party **attack/order** toward the actor (never a dialogue) |

## 5. Movement, pathfinding and the passable/blocked decision

The click itself only **stores** the destination (`Entity+0x0C/+0x0E`, order byte
`+0x02`, `+0x14 = 0xFF`). The canonical "issue a move" routine is
`FUN_004142D4(entity, cellx, celly, action)` (`ev_987d36f8…`):

#### `drawer_konung_general_a823b8a0809fa60a22c74c6c`

- **wing:** konung
- **room:** general
- **source_file:** C:\Users\z10ba\AppData\Local\Temp\opencode\konung-docs-v3\input\MOUSE_INPUT_SYSTEM.md
- **filed_at:** 2026-10-21T20:12:30.750174
- **added_by:** opencode
- **hall:** general
- **chunk_index:** 36
- **chunk_total:** 54

e canonical "issue a move" routine is
`FUN_004142D4(entity, cellx, celly, action)` (`ev_987d36f8…`):

```c
*(u16*)(e+0x0C)=cellx; *(u16*)(e+0x0E)=celly; *(u8*)(e+2)=action; *(u8*)(e+0x14)=0xFF;
if (FUN_0043DA1F(e) == 0) return 0;             /* route failed -> NO move */
*(u8*)(e+2) &= 0xBF;                            /* clear the +0x02 route bit 0x40 */
FUN_0041435C(e, (*(u8*)(e+3) & 4) ? 1 : 7);     /* walk anim (battle/idle) */
return 1;
```

#### `drawer_konung_general_cca4aab573436a65df863fe3`

- **wing:** konung
- **room:** general
- **source_file:** C:\Users\z10ba\AppData\Local\Temp\opencode\konung-docs-v3\input\MOUSE_INPUT_SYSTEM.md
- **filed_at:** 2026-10-21T20:12:30.750174
- **added_by:** opencode
- **hall:** creative
- **chunk_index:** 37
- **chunk_total:** 54

0x40 */
FUN_0041435C(e, (*(u8*)(e+3) & 4) ? 1 : 7);     /* walk anim (battle/idle) */
return 1;
```

**Route building happens on the next per-frame tick, not in the click handler.**
`FUN_00436214` calls the group/actor dispatcher **`FUN_00421B50`**, which routes
each group by class (`Group+0x1E`): `&0x40` → `FUN_004123BC` (object/enemy),
`&0x80` → `FUN_00415FC8` (citizen, delegating movement to `FUN_004168A4`),
`&0xC0==0` → inline per-actor switch. The **walk/idle cases** build the route via
**`FUN_0041ECC8` → `FUN_0043DA1F` → `FUN_0041435C`** (the player/party path),
while the object/enemy path uses `FUN_004142D4`. `FUN_0043DA1F` is the
collision-aware pathfinder (searches the Konung.N collision grid `+0x06400`,
320×160, `index = x*160 + y` over the parity-selected 8-neighbour set; see

#### `drawer_konung_general_2bfcacc9e3b1142c45999354`

- **wing:** konung
- **room:** general
- **source_file:** C:\Users\z10ba\AppData\Local\Temp\opencode\konung-docs-v3\input\MOUSE_INPUT_SYSTEM.md
- **filed_at:** 2026-10-21T20:12:30.751381
- **added_by:** opencode
- **hall:** memory
- **chunk_index:** 38
- **chunk_total:** 54

ollision grid `+0x06400`,
320×160, `index = x*160 + y` over the parity-selected 8-neighbour set; see
`KONUNG_SOURCE_OF_TRUTH.md` §1.1/§3 and AGENTS.md); it is called with the entity
only and reads the stored destination from the struct. So
**the collision decision is made when the unit processes its stored order**, not
in the click handler itself:

- **passable & reachable** → a direction queue is written to `Entity+0x13`, the
  walk animation starts, and the unit steps cell-by-cell;
- **blocked or unreachable** → `FUN_0043DA1F` returns 0 → `FUN_004142D4` returns
  0 → the target stays stored but **no walk starts** (the unit never enters a
  blocked cell and never moves to an unreachable goal).

#### `drawer_konung_general_25a56502441c8a659da924f2`

- **wing:** konung
- **room:** general
- **source_file:** C:\Users\z10ba\AppData\Local\Temp\opencode\konung-docs-v3\input\MOUSE_INPUT_SYSTEM.md
- **filed_at:** 2026-10-21T20:12:30.752387
- **added_by:** opencode
- **hall:** creative
- **chunk_index:** 39
- **chunk_total:** 54

**no walk starts** (the unit never enters a
  blocked cell and never moves to an unreachable goal).

`FUN_00438AB4` (the cursor→cell inverse of the verified projection
`sx = y*58 + (2-(x&1))*29, sy = x*16 + 16`) clamps its output to
grid `[0..0x13F] × [0..0x9F]` (320×160), so an off-map click resolves to the
nearest border cell.

## 6. Globals quick reference

| global | role |
|---|---|
| `_DAT_0081D5B0` | mouse buttons: bit0 left, bit1 right |
| `_DAT_0081D5B4` | held/repeat mirror of the buttons |
| `_DAT_0081D5B8` / `_DAT_0081D5BC` | accumulated mouse dx / dy |
| `_DAT_0081D5C0` / `_DAT_0081D5C4` | integrated, clamped cursor x / y |
| `_DAT_0081D5C8` | frame budget (`0x3C` = 60 ms) |
| `_DAT_0081D5AC` | top-level screen/state id (0 = in-game; 1..9 menus/screens) |

## 7. Original mouse cursor

The game hides the Win32 cursor and draws its own: `LoadCursorA(NULL, 0x7F00)`
(= `IDC_ARROW`) is only the WNDCLASS cursor; `ShowCursor(0)` (`0x42B574`) and
`SetCursor(0); return 1` in WM_SETCURSOR (`0x42B8E0`) hide it.

- **Bitmaps:** 11 × 32×32, the first blocks of `GRAPH.RES` at offset `0x20000`
  (`FUN_004392B4` copies them into the RAM table `0x81A83C`; `res/graph.rs`
  already steps over the same 11 blocks). Format `[u32 type=0x05000000]
  [u32 size]`, then a **16-bit color** sprite `[u16 w][u16 h][h×u16 row-size]
  [rows]`: `0x00` ends a row, `b & 0x80` skips `(b & 0x7F)` pixels, else `b`
  literal 2-byte pixels (`FUN_0043D2FE` copies raw 16-bit words, no palette).

x7F)` pixels, else `b`
  literal 2-byte pixels (`FUN_0043D2FE` copies raw 16-bit words, no palette).
- **Selection `FUN_00428740`** (per frame, gameplay only) returns, in priority,
  `1` over a monster/attackable actor, `5` over a talkable human
  (`dialogue_id != 0xFF`), `6` over a ready friendly, `3` over a non-talkable
  actor or blocked ground, `7` over passable ground; otherwise the base id.
- **Base id `_DAT_0081D660`** is set by `FUN_00424E0C` from the active
  item/action class (`0..=0xC`): `0,2→10` (sword), `1,12→2` (bow), `3..5→0`
  (chest/object), `9→8` (green/location), else `9` (creature). All 11 frames are
  reachable — `1,3,5,6,7` from the hover override plus `0,2,4,8,9,10` from the
  base.
- **Blit `FUN_0042A470`** saves a 32×32 patch (`DAT_00623318`), selects with

**Remake:** `res::cursor` decodes all 11 frames (`res::cursor_image`, accessor
`Res::cursor_sprite`) from `GRAPH.RES`; `app::cursor::cursor_index` ports
`FUN_00428740` (hover) over `cursor_for_class` (`FUN_00424E0C`); `draw_detail`
blits the frame at `cursor_virtual`; `www/app.js` hides the OS cursor in game
mode. Diagnostic export: `tools/cursor_png.mjs` -> `tools/cursor_dump/cursor_NN.png`
(ABI `debug_cursor_count` / `debug_decode_cursor_image`).

All five former open questions were resolved with REA:

1. **`_DAT_0081D660`** is the **base cursor/action id `{0,2,4,7,8,9,10}`** (set by
   `FUN_00424E0C` from the item/action class; `4` = neutral), not a pure order
   state. **`_DAT_0081D5AC`** is the **top-level screen/state id `0..9`** (`-1`
   init `0x42B1B4`, `9` `0x42B2B0`, `0..8` in `FUN_00436214`); `0` = in-game.
2. The click bytes are **order-byte `Entity+0x02`** (`0x48 'H'` = move, bit `0x40`
   route + `0x08`; `0x44 'D'` / `0x41 'A'` = engage, chosen from the active weapon
   slot `+0x12`; `0x60` = halt) with **`Entity+0x03`** flags (bit `0x04` battle

### 9.1 `Entity+0x02` / `Entity+0x03` bit meanings (resolved)

`Entity+0x02` — **order/state byte**:

| bit | meaning | evidence |
|---|---|---|
| `0x01` | **active move/engage order** (low bit of `0x41 'A'`) | `FUN_0041F048` gates on it (`0x41F10F`): clear → actor skipped; set (walk row 1/7) → re-issues the route. `FUN_0041EB48` treats `0x09` as "ordered/busy" |
| `0x02` | **target/follow lock** | `FUN_0041EB48` `OR …,0x2` on acquiring an adjacent enemy; matches the left-click follower marker (`0x4A`) |
| `0x04` | **target/approach mode** | `FUN_0041ECC8` `TEST …,0x4` (clear → walk/idle; set → target branch); toggled by the weapon-slot cycle |
| `0x08` | **move order / arrived** (low bit of `0x48 'H'`) | `FUN_004123BC` clears it on arrival (`&0xF7`) |

| bit | meaning | evidence |
|---|---|---|
| `0x02` | **additional-inventory / bag toggle** | command `0x2D` in `FUN_0042260C` (`0x422F5A`) toggles it + `FUN_0041079C` |
| `0x04` | **battle stance** | pervasive `TEST …,0x4`; selects walk anim 1 vs 7 |
| `0x40` | **arrival / occupy flag** | click clears (left) / sets (right); `FUN_00421B50` toggles it on arrival (`0x422068`) |

# Konung — save, load, configuration and state persistence

Target: `konung.res` (PE32/i386, image base `0x400000`,
`sha256 d698c4f231489a372cc25212ab2819720a36d7ce5307a045db684135cf679810`).
All addresses are virtual addresses in `konung.res`.

Everything in this file was recovered statically. No game process was launched
and no save was created (static-only scope). Field semantics that the code does
not establish are marked **UNKNOWN**.

## 1. File locations

All game files are opened as `path = <data-root> + <literal>`:

- `<data-root>` is the string at `0x81CD30`. It is initialised to an empty string
  and is the same prefix used for `GAME_x`, `QUEST_R.RES`, `FLAMES.RES`,
  `LIGHTS.<loc>` and `KONUNG.<loc>`.
- Because the CD-drive probe (`0x81CC2C`) can leave the CD root empty, the
  effective paths are normally **relative to the process working directory**.

#### `drawer_konung_general_e7c000499c694e9ace5532e8`

- **room:** general
- **source_file:** C:\Users\z10ba\AppData\Local\Temp\opencode\konung-docs-v6\save-load\KONUNG_SAVE_LOAD.md

- **chunk_total:** 22

the CD root empty, the
  effective paths are normally **relative to the process working directory**.

| file | literal | evidence |
|---|---|---|
| configuration | `KONUNG.CFG` @ `0x4502D8` | `ev_9c7da740ee368a1a6202e032edf8b16c84682c1e96ccda2eb21a18c654b444ab` |
| slot-name table | `KONUNG.SAV` @ `0x45020E` / `0x4506AE` | `ev_4ccac98b8699c1eb95ae5f5f13c71b77b61874d67e8c8a47f80b7aea312ee02c` |
| save snapshots | `KONUNG.SA0`..`KONUNG.SA9` @ `0x450219` | `ev_b37503220ddb799cb3598ecf5eb618e0d1e3ce97440586fa4fa2c4031cc0b918` |

The install directory does **not** ship these files; they are created at runtime
by the save screen. The `konung.res` executable itself contains no embedded
save.

## 2. `KONUNG.CFG`

### 2.1 Size and layout

**28 bytes**, seven little-endian `u32` values, no header and no checksum.

| offset | global after read | role | evidence |
|---|---|---|---|
| `+0x00` | `_DAT_0081D644` | video mode: `0x111` = 640×480, `0x114` = 800×600 | WinMain `0x42B3F6`–`0x42B4B2` |

At WinMain `0x42B3D6`:

### 2.2 Read (startup)

At WinMain `0x42B3D6`:

```c
fd = open("KONUNG.CFG", O_RDONLY|O_BINARY);   // CRT wrapper 0x43ECA7, mode 0x200
if (fd != -1) {
    read(fd, &_DAT_0081D644, 4);
    read(fd, &_DAT_0081D5D4, 4);
    read(fd, &_DAT_0081D5EC, 4);
    read(fd, &_DAT_0081D604, 4);
    read(fd, &_DAT_0081D60C, 4);
    read(fd, &_DAT_0081D620, 4);
    read(fd, &_DAT_0081D62C, 4);
    close(fd);
    if (video_mode != 0x111 && video_mode != 0x114)
        video_mode = 0x111;                    // force 640x480 on bad value
} else {
    _DAT_0081D62C = _DAT_0081D620 = _DAT_0081D60C = _DAT_0081D604 =
    _DAT_0081D5EC = _DAT_0081D5D4 = 1;         // all option flags default to 1
    video_mode = 0x111;
}

```

The reads are **not length-checked**; a short file leaves later globals at their
BSS values.

### 2.3 Write (options screen)

Options screen state 6, `0x437B44`–`0x437C24`:

```c
fd = open("KONUNG.CFG", O_RDWR|O_CREAT|O_TRUNC|O_BINARY); // wrapper 0x43F16F, flags 0x180
if (fd != -1) {
    write(fd, &0x831C58, 4);        // options working copy (0x111 / 0x114)
    write(fd, &_DAT_0081D5D4, 4);
    write(fd, &_DAT_0081D5EC, 4);
    write(fd, &_DAT_0081D604, 4);
    write(fd, &_DAT_0081D60C, 4);
    write(fd, &_DAT_0081D620, 4);
    write(fd, &_DAT_0081D62C, 4);
    close(fd);
}
```

The writer opens with the CRT flags observed at `0x43F16F` → `0x43ECC9`
(`CreateFileA` with create/truncate semantics). Failure is silent; there is no
error message and no retry.

## 3. `KONUNG.SAV` — slot-name table

### 3.1 Size and layout

**500 bytes = 10 slots × 50 bytes.** Each slot is a NUL-terminated CP866 string
(the code uses `strcpy`/`strcat`/`strlen`, so it is C-string data, not fixed
binary fields).

#### `drawer_konung_general_6ac80be8b02ee14c0b5e9ed8`

- **wing:** konung
- **room:** general
- **source_file:** C:\Users\z10ba\AppData\Local\Temp\opencode\konung-docs-v6\save-load\KONUNG_SAVE_LOAD.md
- **filed_at:** 2026-10-21T20:29:46.981199
- **added_by:** opencode
- **hall:** consciousness
- **chunk_index:** 8
- **chunk_total:** 22

string
(the code uses `strcpy`/`strcat`/`strlen`, so it is C-string data, not fixed
binary fields).

- Loaded at startup by `FUN_004392B4` (`0x439680`–`0x4396F6`):
  `read(fd, 0x653518, 500)`.
- If the file is missing, all ten slots are filled from the literal
  `"Пустая строка"` @ `0x4506B9` and `_DAT_0081D678 = -1` (no selection).
- If the file exists, `_DAT_0081D678 = 0` initially.
- The save screen writes the whole 500-byte table back (`FUN_00424B3C`, first
  block) after copying the edited name for the selected slot.
- The displayed name is composed at `0x4368C1`–`0x4369BA`:
  `default location name` + `" "` + a formatted date/time string, then the
  formatter output is truncated at the `:` before the seconds field.

#### `drawer_konung_general_ec2be40a2819b8b34efa9c29`

- **wing:** konung
- **room:** general
- **source_file:** C:\Users\z10ba\AppData\Local\Temp\opencode\konung-docs-v6\save-load\KONUNG_SAVE_LOAD.md
- **filed_at:** 2026-10-21T20:29:46.983202
- **added_by:** opencode
- **hall:** identity
- **chunk_index:** 9
- **chunk_total:** 22

tted date/time string, then the
  formatter output is truncated at the `:` before the seconds field.
- Exact format (`FUN_0043F57C` → `FUN_0043F5ED`, an `asctime` clone):
  `Www Mmm dd hh:mm:ss yyyy\n`; leading zero of the day is replaced by a space.
  The save-name loop strips the newline, seconds and year, then removes the
  remaining colon, leaving `Www Mmm dd hh:mm`.
- Example: `Родники Tue Oct 21 20:15`.

### 3.2 Default names

`0x45B394` is a table of CP866 default save names, stride `0x28` (40 bytes).
The save screen builds the selected name from
`0x45B394 + (current_location - 1) * 0x28` and appends a space
(`0x4368D0`–`0x4368FB`). Examples at the table base:

- entry 0 (used for location 1): `Родники`
- next entry: `Скромное место`

#### `drawer_konung_general_b1682da22864005145295c3a`

- **wing:** konung
- **room:** general
- **source_file:** C:\Users\z10ba\AppData\Local\Temp\opencode\konung-docs-v6\save-load\KONUNG_SAVE_LOAD.md
- **filed_at:** 2026-10-21T20:29:46.985208
- **added_by:** opencode
- **hall:** identity
- **chunk_index:** 10
- **chunk_total:** 22

amples at the table base:

- entry 0 (used for location 1): `Родники`
- next entry: `Скромное место`

The table is only a **source of a default display name**; the actual slot text
is the buffer `0x81CE34`, which is copied into `KONUNG.SAV[slot]` when saving.

### 3.3 Slot selection

`_DAT_0081D678` is the selected slot, `0..9`:

- `-1` = nothing selected / no `KONUNG.SAV`;
- `0..9` = selected row in the 10-row list.

The snapshot filename is `KONUNG.SA` + `('0' + _DAT_0081D678)`, i.e.
`KONUNG.SA0` through `KONUNG.SA9`. This is implemented by
`strlen(path); path[last] += _DAT_0081D678` at `0x424BE8` (save) and
`0x42475F` (load).

## 4. `KONUNG.SA0`..`SA9` — snapshot format

### 4.1 Exact layout

The writer `FUN_00424B3C` and reader `FUN_004246C0` use the same sequential
order. All values are little-endian. The file has no magic, version, checksum or
length prefix.

| offset | size | source/target global | meaning |
|---|---|---|---|
| `0x000000` | `u32` | `_DAT_0081D5EC` | option flag 2 |
| `0x000004` | `u32` | `_DAT_0081D604` | option flag 3 |
| `0x000008` | `u32` | `_DAT_0081D640` | world/frame tick counter |
| `0x00000C` | `u32` | leader entity index | `(leader_ptr - 0x78A250) / 0x100` |
| `0x000010` | `u32` | `_DAT_0081D6A0` | active projectile/effect count (`0..100`) |
| `0x000014` | `u32` | `_DAT_0081D6A8` | active ground-marker count |

- **added_by:** opencode

- **chunk_index:** 12
- **chunk_total:** 22

tile/effect count (`0..100`) |
| `0x000014` | `u32` | `_DAT_0081D6A8` | active ground-marker count |
| `0x000018` | `0x4B0` | `0x615068` | quest flags, 300 × `u32` |
| `0x0004C8` | `0xC80` | `0x80B0D0` | projectile/effect pool, 100 × `0x20` |
| `0x001148` | `0x20000` | `0x659B0C` | item class/subtype table (GAME_x block) |
| `0x021148` | `0x3200` | `0x785BB4` | 200 × 0x40 ground-marker records |
| `0x024348` | `0x1900` | `0x67C08C` | 400 × 0x10 actor name/record table |
| `0x025C48` | `0x7D00` | `0x77DEB4` | 500 × 0x40 WorldObject records |
| `0x02D948` | `0x7D000` | `0x78A250` | 2000 × 0x100 Entity records |
| `0x0AA948` | `0x5E24` | `0x8149F0` | 20 × 0x4B5 settlement records |
| `0x0B076C` | `0x38` | `0x81CF80` | 56 discovered world-map location flags |

The four leading fields (16 bytes) plus the leader record are read first; the
remaining blocks are read in order from offset `0x10`.

### 4.2 Writer `FUN_00424B3C` (`0x424B3C`–`0x424D8B`)

Evidence: `ev_ff1ece81f670f72c2287b52109fdfde0829704de445d9f957f6b36c44513a75b`.

```text
1. open("<data-root>KONUNG.SAV", create/truncate) and write 0x653518[500].
2. open("<data-root>KONUNG.SA" + slot, create/truncate).
3. write header:
     _DAT_0081D5EC, _DAT_0081D604, _DAT_0081D640, leader_index.
4. write _DAT_0081D6A0, _DAT_0081D6A8.
5. write the 12 blocks listed above (quest flags .. 0x81CF80).
6. close.
```

#### `drawer_konung_general_2e3e5f8e8be029457cbe3600`

- **wing:** konung
- **room:** general
- **source_file:** C:\Users\z10ba\AppData\Local\Temp\opencode\konung-docs-v6\save-load\KONUNG_SAVE_LOAD.md
- **filed_at:** 2026-10-21T20:29:46.991732
- **added_by:** opencode
- **hall:** memory
- **chunk_index:** 14
- **chunk_total:** 22

081D6A0, _DAT_0081D6A8.
5. write the 12 blocks listed above (quest flags .. 0x81CF80).
6. close.
```

`leader_index` is computed from the pointer difference
`(_DAT_0081D56C - 0x78A250) / 0x100`, with the compiler's signed-division
correction. The leader is always `Entity[0]` in the shipped party data, but the
format stores the index explicitly.

No return value is produced; open failures are silently ignored.
The **first** open (`KONUNG.SAV`) and the snapshot open are independent: a
failure of one does not abort the other.

Evidence: `ev_82b6a0cf7fba776a112c2b13d3a2e85b330457931c2e606ee08e048556bf90e8`.

```text
1. Clear: _81D560, _81D5BC, _81D5B8, _81D630, _81D624, _81D610,
          _81D600, _81D5E4, _81D568.
2. path = <data-root> + "KONUNG.SA" + slot; open read-only binary.
3. If open fails -> return 0 (save screen returns to menu page 4).
4. Read 16-byte header; set leader = 0x78A250 + index*0x100;
   clear the 10-entry selection array and set selection[0] = leader.
5. FUN_0043A75C()   -> load FLAMES.RES.
6. Save current file position; seek to
      0x10 + 0x2D938 + index*0x100   (= 0x2D948 + index*0x100)
   and read 0x100 bytes into 0x81D45C (startup leader copy);
   seek back.
7. FUN_0043A870()   -> load GAME_x + QUEST_R.RES from the install data.
